The new revision must do more than shorten sentences. Model a teacher thinking beside a student: explain an ordinary meaning, follow its consequences, pause at a genuine decision, examine the tempting alternative, and settle the reasoning before moving on. Vary sentence length naturally; use questions with a purpose and contractions without forced slang. Define conditions and explain why the relationship matters before an example or notation. Develop sufficient and necessary in separate teaching beats; revisit the same original ready/packed rule to isolate the difference. Show why enough need not be the only way, and why required need not be enough. Introduce technical names after ordinary meaning. Keep every full target and limiting modifier.

Expand Group 1 and Group 2 into visible explanation subsections for all seven expressions, followed by their existing target laboratory, fresh diagram work and recall. Explain meaning and clause order before left/right placement. Explain the difference between `only` and `the only` through the same relationship. Treat `must` as a requirement, never assumed compliance. Give the learner a sensible transition from notes to the attached guided activity, then to recorded fresh practice. No teacher assignment jargon or difficulty controls on lessons. Do not mechanically reproduce the source's jokes, examples, four-step routine or exact language.

## What must work

- Ground-up progression modeled on current Intro to LR: what conditionals are and why they matter, sufficient, necessary, then full Group 1 and Group 2 phases.
- Each group has actual sublessons: meaning, worked examples, indicator target, diagram, guided practice, independent practice, consolidation. Seven current indicators per group, complete modifiers and clause order included.
- Use Joshua's teaching voice, readable condition phrases, What Test. Preserve teacher assignments and current unit IDs. No student difficulty selector, assigned-stage or teaching-sequence jargon.
- Every visualization teaches a relation or limit: containment, fact-to-rule application, alternate permitted case, enough versus required, indicator-to-target alignment, if versus only if, only versus the only. Motion responds to learner actions and reduced motion. Keyboard equivalents for visual controls.
- Real answer-specific feedback and flashcards, with no fake mastery or claim to grade unassessed free-form logic.
- Responsive high-quality visual hierarchy, meaningful SVG illustrations, whitespace and varied composition. User explicitly overrides skill's exact color-theme matching requirement.
- Reuse adequate existing activities. Source-generated media are optional reinforcement, never prerequisites; show only completed reviewed assets. No placeholder media links or autoplay.
- Scope ends at Group 2. Do not silently teach Groups 3/4, quantity, chaining, or full contraposition. Sandra 2026-09-28 teaches a five-step method (identify indicator; label ideas; apply side rule; contrapositive; read both in English). Older notes use six steps. Explain diagramming within opening; preserve evolution in internal evidence, don't invent a canonical five-step method.

# Claude voice and conditional revision review

2026-10-02. Codex builds; Claude reviews and independently inspects. CLI default requested; observed first-review model claude-opus-5-5, Claude Code 2.1.280. Two plan rounds, two inspection rounds maximum. No publication or student assignment.

## Plan round 1

Native CLI review returned REVISE, with six findings. The result was invalidated because the coordinator amended the plan during the run. That was a coordination mistake; no approval claimed, no app editing started. The first PATH launch also failed before Claude started; use the verified native executable. Native result: claude-voice-application-plan-1-retry/claudex-g2c137z6/result.json, full response retained.

### Host dispositions for round 2

- F1 accepted: reconcile in the shared QuestionRunner for unit, area, phase and flashcard pools; preserve drafts and attempts. Add route and pure helper tests. Three changed semantic IDs receive v2 identifiers; exact HEAD historical questions remain resolvable for old review.
- F2 accepted: every in-lesson Check requires feedback for each option, naming the exact mistake. Add behavior coverage for each group.
- F3 accepted: remove extra-indicator reference paragraphs; maintain scope and guard catalogue/process wording in teaching.
- F4 accepted: preserve read flags; show a review-expanded-lesson link during changed-pool practice.
- F5 accepted for generated fallback titles: remove level number wording. Preserve explicit existing teacher-authored row titles; no change to teacher controls or assigned level enforcement.
- F6 accepted: browser proof concerns actual lesson components only. Route/persistence tests prove recorded practice logic, not an authenticated production browser session.

Round 2 uses a fresh review session because runner refuses resuming a failed result. The plan is frozen during this review. Read the revised contract and the full canonical voice standard. Focus review on the explicit corrections and any remaining material gaps.

## Plan round 2

Actual Claude Code returned REVISE. Result: C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\brainstorms\2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum\claude-voice-application-plan-2\claudex-h1van3uj\result.json

Round 2 handles all six round-1 findings at the plan level. The voice direction matches the canonical standard: ordinary meaning before notation, sufficient and necessary taught in separate beats on one anchor, complete targets, must treated as a requirement, and scope ending at Group 2. Four integration problems remain in the code the plan touches. (1) The planned QuestionRunner reconciliation would compare saved runs against pools whose saved-progress keys don't record which assignment they came from, so switching assignments would reopen or reshape finished runs. (2) The F5 title fix would change nothing for existing students, because titles and descriptions are stored on each assignment row when it's created. (3) The planned link back to the expanded lesson depends on a condition that disappears once reconciliation runs, and it ignores practice-only gating. (4) The review screen that would need the historical registry is not named. Smaller gaps: ordering and no-op rules for reconciliation, a runtime check that each option has feedback, an ambiguity about whether rules-facts is in scope, and a pre-existing MCP file that must stay out of any commit.

- R2-1 (high): Practice (routes.tsx:210-211) and Flashcards (routes.tsx:259-260) filter questions by selection.allowedUnits. Their run keys (`practice:${difficulty}:${unitId ?? area ?? phase}`, `flashcards:${unitId ?? area ?? 'conditional'}:${level}`) contain neither the assignment ID nor the allowed units. Admin pathSelection leaves allowedUnits undefined, so admins get the full pool under the same keys. Today a pool mismatch only shows the 'no longer in this set' notice, and only when the current item is missing. Under the plan, reconciliation would add every ID in the active pool and drop every ID outside it. Failure scenario: a student finishes the translation area run under assignment A, which allows group-1 and group-2. They then open assignment B, which allows every translation unit. Reconciliation adds the extra units' items and sets finished to false. When they return to A, those items are dropped again and the cursor moves. The run is rewritten on every assignment switch, even though no content changed.
  Requested fix: Reconcile against content revisions, not against whatever pool is active. Add a small revision registry: retiredIds (group-1-a3, group-2-m2, group-2-a3) mapped to their v2 IDs, plus the list of newly added opening IDs. Only drop registered retired IDs, and only add registered new IDs whose unit and difficulty already appear in the run. Never drop or add an ID just because the assignment filter changed. Add a route test where two assignments share an area run key with different allowedUnits, and check that the run stays unchanged when switching between them.
- R2-2 (medium): ConditionalRun stores questionIds in order plus an index (progress.ts:23-32). parseConditionalProgress rejects any index greater than questionIds.length (progress.ts:57). The accepted reordering puts the opening units first, which changes the order getConditionalQuestions returns for phase and area pools while keeping the same set of IDs. If reconciliation compares ordered arrays, it would rewrite unchanged sets and move the cursor. The finished-set summary counts every attempt whose ID starts with run.id (routes.tsx:231-234). After retired IDs leave the active sequence, a reconciled 9-item set could report something like '10 checked answers'. The 'Next question' handler advances index+1 even when that item is already checked, so spliced-in new IDs make resume order unclear.
  Requested fix: Define reconciliation as order-insensitive. If the set of IDs is unchanged, return the same object so no save is triggered. Keep the saved order for retained IDs and append new IDs at the end. Set index to the first unchecked retained or new ID, and set finished to false only if one exists. Keep run.id so existing attempt IDs stay linked. Count the summary over attempts for IDs in the active questionIds only, or label retired attempts separately. Add tests: reordered but unchanged set (no write), completed old run, in-progress run with checked and unchecked drafts, and an index that stays valid under parseConditionalProgress.
- R2-3 (medium): The F4 disposition shows the review-expanded-lesson link 'when their saved practice pool needs reconciliation'. Reconciliation writes progress in an effect when the runner mounts. After the first save, the run no longer needs reconciling, so the link appears for one render or never. Practice-only assignments also block lessons: conditionalAssignmentAllowsAction returns false for non-practice actions unless mode is 'learn' (assignments.ts:80), and UnitActions hides lesson links for practiceOnly (routes.tsx:153). A link shown to those students would lead to a gated route.
  Requested fix: Base the condition on stored data that persists: the unit is one of the four opening units, and its lessons[unit.id] timestamp is earlier than a constant expanded-lesson revision date. That needs no schema change and leaves read flags alone. Hide the link when selection.practiceOnly is true. Add a test covering an old read date, a new read date, and practice-only.
- R2-4 (medium): assignWork.ts:329-330 stores conditional.title and conditional.description on the classroom row when the assignment is created. resolveConditionalAssignments uses `row.title || resolved.title` (assignments.ts:69). As a result, every existing conditional assignment already stores a generated title such as 'Group 1: ...: Level 2 practice', and the plan can't tell it apart from a title a teacher typed. Changing only the fallback affects no existing student. The stored description also includes 'Assigned level 2 practice' (conditionalBootcampAssignment.ts:76), which F5 does not cover. The exported recommendNext still builds a `Level ${level} activity` label (progress.ts:74).
  Requested fix: Remove level numbers from both the generated title and the generated description. For existing rows, at render time in resolveConditionalAssignments, rebuild the old generated title and description for that row's target. When row.title or row.description exactly matches the old string, show the new wording; leave anything else unchanged, since a teacher must have edited it. Remove or neutralize the Level label in progress.ts:74. Test an old generated title, a teacher-edited title, and the description.
- R2-5 (medium): The review screen looks up a question only through `unit.guidedExample` or `unit.drills[attempt.difficulty].find(...)` (routes.tsx:272) and then renders `question.options[attempt.choice]`. The plan promises a historical-only registry 'used when reviewing old attempts' but never names this lookup. If the HEAD questions are removed from drills and this lookup isn't changed, old attempts on group-1-a3, group-2-m2 and group-2-a3 show 'The authored question is unavailable'. If the lookup somehow resolved to the v2 question, the saved choice index would be read against different options. That second outcome is exactly the change to stored answer meaning the plan forbids.
  Requested fix: Route every attempt-to-question lookup, including the review screen and any review or export helpers, through a single resolver: current drills and guided example first, then the historical registry by exact ID. The registry must never feed getConditionalQuestions, nextSelectedStep or any runner pool. Test that the review screen shows each of the three exact HEAD prompts and options with the saved choice, and that none of them appears in a newly created run.
- R2-6 (low): Check is typed `feedback?: string[]` (ConditionalOpeningLesson.tsx:21). F2 makes feedback required. A required string[] still accepts an array shorter than options or containing blank entries, so the wrong-choice message could be undefined.
  Requested fix: Require feedback to have one entry per option, either with a tuple-typed helper or a dev or test assertion that feedback.length equals options.length and every entry is non-empty. Add a content test that walks every opening Check and verifies this.
- R2-7 (low): The working tree gives rules-facts the prerequisite ['group-2'] (foundations.source.ts:61), and the 62-question count (9+9+23+21) covers only orientation, sufficient-necessary, group-1 and group-2. The 'Accepted review corrections' section still maps 'rule versus fact/direction to rules-facts' as one of the opening units placed before truth-validity. The plan never says whether rules-facts is in the voice revision's scope or where the fact-to-rule teaching now lives.
  Requested fix: State the actual opening order (orientation, sufficient-necessary, group-1, group-2, then rules-facts) and say that rules-facts is outside this authoring scope. Also state that the fact-to-rule application required by the voice standard is taught inside orientation.
- R2-8 (low): This pre-existing modified file imports `npm:C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\lsatu-conditional-bootcamp\src\lib\mcp\index.ts` (line 6), a local Windows absolute path that would break any deployment. The plan only says to preserve it.
  Requested fix: Keep the hash check, and also state that this file and the other two protected files must be left out of any commit or diff produced for this revision. Final inspection should confirm they are absent from the change set.

Host accepts R2-1 through R2-8; concrete dispositions recorded in the contract. Plan round budget is exhausted. No APPROVED result or approval check is claimed. Joshua explicitly requests implementation and final Claude review; host continues that authorized work, with this departure from the plan-approval workflow recorded. The final inspection remains independent and mandatory.

Finishing a set does not certify mastery.</p><div className="cb-actions"><Link className="cb-button" to={`${BASE}/review${pathUrl(forwardSelection ?? pathSelection(new URLSearchParams())).replace(BASE, '')}`}>Review your attempts</Link><button onClick={() => replaceRun(() => newRun(questions.map(item => item.id)))}><RotateCcw size={16} /> Repeat this set</button></div><RecommendedForward selection={forwardSelection} /></>;
256	  }

263	  });

265	  const checkAnswer = () => update(previous => {

271	  });
272	  return <>{expandedLessonReview}<div className="cb-actions"><span className="cb-label">{run.index + 1} / {run.questionIds.length} · {guidedMethod ? 'Guided activity' : "Skill drill"}</span>{question.difficulty !== 'beginner' && <><button onClick={() => { replaceRun(value => ({ ...value, timerEnabled: true })); setTimerNow(Date.now()); replaceRun(value => value.timerStartedAt ? { ...value, elapsed: elapsedSeconds(value), timerStartedAt: null } : { ...value, timerStartedAt: new Date().toISOString() }); }}><Clock size={16} /> {run.timerStartedAt ? 'Pause timing' : 'Start optional timing'}</button>{run.timerEnabled && <span aria-label="Elapsed time">{Math.floor(elapsedSeconds(run, timerNow) / 60)}:{String(elapsedSeconds(run, timerNow) % 60).padStart(2, '0')}</span>}</>}</div>{!hideSkill && <p className="cb-muted">{question.unitTitle}</p>}<h3>{question.prompt}</h3>{guidedMethod && <div className="cb-reason"><h4>Build your response in these steps</h4><ol>{guidedMethod.map(step => <li key={step}>{step}</li>)}</ol><p>Identify the conditions. 1	import type { ConditionalDifficulty, ConditionalUnit } from './content/types';
2	import { getCurriculumDifficulty, getCurriculumUnits } from './curriculumPath';
3	import { openingQuestionRevisions } from './openingRevision';

8	  revealed: boolean;

10	  checked: boolean;

15	  unitId: string;
16	  difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty;
17	  choice: number | null;
18	  correct: boolean;

24	export interface ConditionalRun {
25	  id: string;
26	  questionIds: string[];
27	  index: number;
28	  drafts: Record<string, AnswerDraft>;
29	  finished: boolean;
30	  elapsed: number;
31	  timerEnabled: boolean;
32	  timerStartedAt?: string | null;
33	}
34	export interface ConditionalProgress {
35	  id: 'conditionals-v1';
36	  lessons: Record<string, string>;
37	  runs: Record<string, ConditionalRun>;
38	  attempts: ConditionalAttempt[];
39	  journal: string;
40	  settings: { showMethod: boolean };
41	  updated_at: string;
42	}
43	export const emptyDraft = (): AnswerDraft => ({ working: '', hint: false, revealed: false, choice: null, checked: false });
44	export const emptyProgress = (): ConditionalProgress => ({ id: 'conditionals-v1', lessons: {}, runs: {}, attempts: [], journal: '', settings: { showMethod: true }, updated_at: new Date().toISOString() });
45	export function newRun(questionIds: string[]): ConditionalRun {
46	  return { id: crypto.randomUUID(), questionIds, index: 0, drafts: {}, finished: false, elapsed: 0, timerEnabled: false };
47	}
48	function hasCheckedQuestion(run: ConditionalRun, questionId: string, attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]): boolean {
49	  return !!run.drafts[questionId]?.checked || attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === questionId && attempt.id.startsWith(`${run.id}:`));
50	}
51	export function nextUncheckedQuestionIndex(run: ConditionalRun, attempts: ConditionalAttempt[], after = -1): number {
52	  const pending = (id: string) => !hasCheckedQuestion(run, id, attempts);
53	  const next = run.questionIds.findIndex((id, index) => index > after && pending(id));
54	  return next >= 0 ? next : run.questionIds.findIndex(pending);
55	}
56	export function reconcileConditionalRun(run: ConditionalRun, questions: { id: string; unitId: string; difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty }[], attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]): ConditionalRun {
57	  const savedIds = new Set(run.questionIds);
58	  const removedIds = new Set<string>();
59	  const addedIds: string[] = [];
60	  for (const revision of openingQuestionRevisions) {
61	    if (![...revision.previousIds, ...revision.addedIds].some(id => savedIds.has(id))) continue;
62	    const allowedIds = new Set(questions.filter(question => question.unitId === revision.unitId && question.difficulty === revision.difficulty).map(question => question.id));
63	    for (const [retiredId, replacementId] of Object.entries(revision.replacements)) {
64	      if (savedIds.has(retiredId) && allowedIds.has(replacementId)) removedIds.add(retiredId);
65	    }
66	    for (const id of revision.addedIds) if (allowedIds.has(id) && !savedIds.has(id)) addedIds.push(id);
67	  }
68	  if (!removedIds.size && !addedIds.length) return run;
69	  const questionIds = [...run.questionIds.filter(id => !removedIds.has(id)), ...addedIds];
70	  const currentId = run.questionIds[run.index];
71	  const currentIndex = questionIds.indexOf(currentId);
72	  const keepCurrent = !run.finished && currentIndex >= 0 && questions.some(question => question.id === currentId);
73	  const updated = { ...run, questionIds };
74	  const pendingIndex = nextUncheckedQuestionIndex(updated, attempts);
75	  const index = keepCurrent ? currentIndex : pendingIndex >= 0 ? pendingIndex : questionIds.length;
76	  return { ...updated, index, finished: !keepCurrent && pendingIndex < 0 };
77	}
78	export function attemptSummary(attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]) {
79	  return { total: attempts.length, correct: attempts.filter(a => a.correct).length, assisted: attempts.filter(a => a.hint || a.explanationBeforeCheck).length };
80	}
81	export function parseConditionalProgress(value: unknown): ConditionalProgress {
82	  const bad = () => { throw new Error('Your stored conditional progress has an unsupported format. Your record has been preserved; ask Joshua to review it.'); };
83	  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object') return bad();
84	  const record = value as ConditionalProgress;
85	  if (record.id !== 'conditionals-v1' || !record.lessons || typeof record.lessons !== 'object' || !record.runs || typeof record.runs !== 'object' || !Array.isArray(record.attempts) || typeof record.journal !== 'string' || typeof record.settings?.showMethod !== 'boolean' || !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(record.updated_at))) return bad();
86	  if (Object.values(record.lessons).some(date => typeof date !== 'string' || !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(date)))) return bad();
87	  for (const run of Object.values(record.runs)) {
88	    if (!run || typeof run.id !== 'string' || !Array.isArray(run.questionIds) || run.questionIds.some(id => typeof id !== 'string') || !Number.isInteger(run.index) || run.index < 0 || run.index > run.questionIds.length || !run.drafts || typeof run.drafts !== 'object' || typeof run.finished !== 'boolean' || typeof run.timerEnabled !== 'boolean' || !Number.isFinite(run.elapsed) || run.elapsed < 0 || (run.timerStartedAt != null && !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(run.timerStartedAt)))) return bad();
89	    for (const draft of Object.values(run.drafts)) if (!draft || typeof draft.working !== 'string' || typeof draft.hint !== 'boolean' || typeof draft.revealed !== 'boolean' || typeof draft.checked !== 'boolean' || (draft.choice !== null && (!Number.isInteger(draft.choice) || draft.choice < 0))) return bad();
90	  }
91	  for (const attempt of record.attempts) if (!attempt || typeof attempt.id !== 'string' || typeof attempt.questionId !== 'string' || typeof attempt.unitId !== 'string' || !['beginner', 'medium', 'advanced'].includes(attempt.difficulty) || typeof attempt.correct !== 'boolean' || typeof attempt.hint !== 'boolean' || typeof attempt.explanationBeforeCheck !== 'boolean' || typeof attempt.working !== 'string' || !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(attempt.checkedAt)) || (attempt.choice !== null && (!Number.isInteger(attempt.choice) || attempt.choice < 0))) return bad();
92	  return record;
93	}
94	export function elapsedSeconds(run: ConditionalRun, now = Date.now()): number {
95	  return run.elapsed + (run.timerStartedAt ? Math.max(0, Math.floor((now - Date.parse(run.timerStartedAt)) / 1000)) : 0);
96	}
97	export function recommendNext(progress: ConditionalProgress, units: ConditionalUnit[], startingUnitId?: string) {
98	  for (const unit of getCurriculumUnits(units, startingUnitId)) {
99	    const difficulty = getCurriculumDifficulty(unit);
100	    const phase = unit.phase;
101	    if (!progress.lessons[unit.id]) return { mode: 'learn' as const, unitId: unit.id, label: `Learn: ${unit.title}`, difficulty, phase };
102	    if (!progress.attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === unit.guidedExample.id)) return { mode: 'learn' as const, unitId: unit.id, label: `Guided example: ${unit.title}`, difficulty, phase };
103	    if (unit.drills[difficulty].some(question => !progress.attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === question.id && attempt.difficulty === difficulty))) {
104	      return { mode: 'practice' as const, unitId: unit.id, label: `Skill drill: ${unit.title}`, difficulty, phase };
105	    }
106	  }
107	  return null;
108	}

1	import { conditionalUnits, findConditionalUnit } from './content';
2	import { DIFFICULTIES, type ConditionalDifficulty } from './content/types';
3	import { getCurriculumDifficulty, getCurriculumUnits } from './curriculumPath';
4	import { CONDITIONAL_AREAS, DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET, conditionalAssignmentDisplayCopy, resolveConditionalAssignment, type ConditionalAssignmentTarget } from '@/lib/conditionalBootcampAssignment';
5	import { classroomItemMatchesBootcamp } from '@/lib/bootcamps';

7	export interface ConditionalAssignmentRow {
8	  id: string; student_id: string; type: string; title: string; description?: string | null;
9	  source_id: string | null; metadata_json: Record<string, unknown> | null;
10	  status: string; visible_to_student: boolean; unlock_at: string | null;
11	}
12	export interface ResolvedConditionalAssignment {
13	  id: string; title: string; description?: string; route: string; target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget;
14	  unitIds: string[]; mode: 'learn' | 'practice'; difficulty?: ConditionalDifficulty;
15	}
16	function parseTarget(value: unknown): ConditionalAssignmentTarget | null {
17	  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) return null;
18	  const target = value as ConditionalAssignmentTarget;
19	  if (!['course', 'stage', 'area', 'skill', 'general'].includes(target.scope) ||
20	    !['learn', 'practice'].includes(target.mode) || !DIFFICULTIES.includes(target.difficulty) ||
21	    (target.curriculumVersion !== undefined && target.curriculumVersion !== 2)) return null;
22	  if ((target.scope === 'skill' || target.scope === 'stage') && !findConditionalUnit(target.unitId)) return null;
23	  if (target.scope === 'area' && !CONDITIONAL_AREAS.some(area => area.id === target.area)) return null;
24	  return target;
25	}
26	function legacyTarget(source: string | null): ConditionalAssignmentTarget | null {
27	  if (!source || !source.startsWith('/bootcamp/conditionals')) return null;
28	  const url = new URL(source, 'https://local.invalid');
29	  const base = '/bootcamp/conditionals';
30	  if (url.pathname !== base && !url.pathname.startsWith(`${base}/`)) return null;
31	  const level = url.searchParams.get('level') ?? 'beginner';
32	  if (!DIFFICULTIES.includes(level as ConditionalDifficulty)) return null;
33	  const difficulty = level as ConditionalDifficulty;
34	  const start = url.searchParams.get('start');
35	  if (start) return parseTarget({ scope: 'stage', mode: 'learn', difficulty, unitId: start });
36	  const pathUnit = url.pathname.match(/^\/bootcamp\/conditionals\/(?:lessons|activities)\/([^/]+)$/)?.[1];
37	  const unitId = pathUnit ?? url.searchParams.get('unit');
38	  const area = url.searchParams.get('area');
39	  const mode = url.pathname === `${base}/practice` ? 'practice' : 'learn';
40	  if (unitId) return parseTarget({ scope: 'skill', mode, difficulty, unitId });
41	  if (area) return parseTarget({ scope: 'area', mode, difficulty, area });
42	  if (url.pathname === `${base}/practice`) return parseTarget({ scope: 'general', mode: 'practice', difficulty });
43	  return url.pathname === base && !url.search ? { ...DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET } : null;
44	}
45	export function conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget): string[] {
46	  if (target.scope === 'course') return getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits).map(unit => unit.id);
47	  if (target.scope === 'stage') {
48	    if (target.curriculumVersion === 2) return getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits, target.unitId).map(unit => unit.id);
49	    // Existing stage grants refer to the original membership, not a mutable display order.
50	    const originalOpening = ['orientation', 'rules-facts', 'sufficient-necessary', 'truth-validity', 'negation', 'contraposition', 'group-1', 'group-2'];
51	    const originalIds = [...originalOpening, ...conditionalUnits.map(unit => unit.id).filter(id => !originalOpening.includes(id))];
52	    const start = originalIds.indexOf(target.unitId);
53	    return originalIds.slice(Math.max(0, start));
54	  }
55	  return conditionalUnits.filter(unit => target.scope === 'skill' ? unit.id === target.unitId
56	    : target.scope === 'area' ? unit.area === target.area : unit.phase === 'conditional').map(unit => unit.id);
57	}
58	export async function resolveConditionalAssignments(rows: ConditionalAssignmentRow[], userId: string, now = Date.now()): Promise<ResolvedConditionalAssignment[]> {
59	  const result: ResolvedConditionalAssignment[] = [];
60	  for (const row of rows) {
61	    if (row.student_id !== userId || row.type !== 'bootcamp_module' || !row.visible_to_student ||
62	      !['assigned', 'in_progress', 'completed'].includes(row.status) || !classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(row, 'conditionals')) continue;
63	    if (row.unlock_at && (!Number.isFinite(Date.parse(row.unlock_at)) || Date.parse(row.unlock_at) > now)) continue;
64	    const metadata = row.metadata_json;
65	    const target = metadata && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(metadata, 'conditional_target')
66	      ? parseTarget(metadata.conditional_target) : legacyTarget(row.source_id);
67	    if (!target) continue;
68	    const resolved = await resolveConditionalAssignment(target);
69	    result.push({ id: row.id, ...conditionalAssignmentDisplayCopy(resolved.target, resolved, row), route: resolved.route, target: resolved.target,
70	      unitIds: conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(resolved.target), mode: resolved.target.mode,
71	      ...(target.scope !== 'course' && target.scope !== 'stage' ? { difficulty: resolved.target.difficulty } : {}) });
72	  }
73	  return result;
74	}
75	/** URL parameters never grant work. Call this against a target read from the student's assignment row. */
76	export function conditionalAssignmentAllowsAction(assignment: ResolvedConditionalAssignment, request: {
77	  unitId: string; action: 'lesson' | 'guided' | 'practice'; difficulty?: ConditionalDifficulty;
78	}): boolean {
79	  if (!assignment.unitIds.includes(request.unitId)) return false;
80	  if (request.action !== 'practice') return assignment.mode === 'learn';
81	  return request.difficulty === (assignment.difficulty ?? getCurriculumDifficulty(request.unitId));
82	}
83	export async function conditionalTeacherPreview(): Promise<ResolvedConditionalAssignment> {
84	  const resolved = await resolveConditionalAssignment();
85	  return { id: 'teacher-preview', title: resolved.title, route: resolved.route, target: resolved.target,
86	    unitIds: conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(resolved.target), mode: 'learn' };
87	}

1	import type { ConditionalArea, ConditionalDifficulty } from '@/bootcamps/conditionals/content/types';
2	
3	export interface ConditionalAssignmentTarget {
4	  scope: 'course' | 'stage' | 'area' | 'skill' | 'general';
5	  mode: 'learn' | 'practice';
6	  difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty;
7	  unitId?: string;
8	  area?: ConditionalArea;
9	  /** Unmarked stage grants keep their original membership. New grants use this teaching order. */
10	  curriculumVersion?: 2;
11	}
12	export const DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET: ConditionalAssignmentTarget = {
13	  scope: 'course', mode: 'learn', difficulty: 'beginner',
14	};
15	export const CONDITIONAL_AREAS: { id: ConditionalArea; title: string }[] = [
16	  { id: 'understanding', title: 'Understanding rules' },
17	  { id: 'translation', title: 'Translating language' },
18	  { id: 'linking', title: 'Connecting rules' },
19	  { id: 'inference', title: 'Using rules with facts' },
20	  { id: 'complex', title: 'Complex conditionals' },
21	  { id: 'application', title: 'Logical Reasoning applications' },
22	  { id: 'quantifiers', title: 'Quantifier reasoning' },
23	];
24	export function conditionalTargetKey(target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget = DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET): string {
25	  if (target.scope === 'course') return 'course';
26	  if (target.scope === 'stage') return target.curriculumVersion === 2 ? `stage:v2:${target.unitId}` : `stage:${target.unitId}`;
27	  return [target.scope, target.scope === 'skill' ? target.unitId : target.scope === 'area' ? target.area : 'conditional',
28	    target.scope === 'general' ? 'practice' : target.mode, target.difficulty].join(':');
29	}
30	export async function resolveConditionalAssignment(target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget = DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET) {
31	  if (!['course', 'stage', 'area', 'skill', 'general'].includes(target.scope) ||
32	      !['learn', 'practice'].includes(target.mode) ||
33	      !['beginner', 'medium', 'advanced'].includes(target.difficulty) ||
34	      (target.curriculumVersion !== undefined && target.curriculumVersion !== 2)) {
35	    throw new Error('Choose a valid conditional scope, learning mode and difficulty.');
36	  }
37	  const base = '/bootcamp/conditionals';
38	  if (target.scope === 'course') return {
39	    route: base, title: 'Conditional Logic Bootcamp',
40	    description: 'The teaching sequence from the beginning, with activity levels set at each stage. Quantifiers follow conditionals.',
41	    target: { ...DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET }, key: 'course',
42	  };
43	  if (target.scope === 'stage') {
44	    const { findConditionalUnit } = await import('@/bootcamps/conditionals/content');
45	    const unit = findConditionalUnit(target.unitId);
46	    if (!unit) throw new Error('Choose an existing conditional starting stage.');
47	    const normalized: ConditionalAssignmentTarget = { scope: 'stage', mode: 'learn', difficulty: 'beginner', unitId: unit.id,
48	      ...(target.curriculumVersion === 2 ? { curriculumVersion: 2 } : {}) };
49	    return {
50	      route: `${base}?${new URLSearchParams({ start: unit.id })}`,
51	      title: `Conditional curriculum: start with ${unit.title}`,
52	      description: 'Start at the assigned skill and continue through the curriculum. Each activity has its own level.',
53	      target: normalized, key: conditionalTargetKey(normalized),
54	    };
55	  }
56	  const params = new URLSearchParams({ level: target.difficulty });
57	  let label = 'General conditional practice';
58	  if (target.scope === 'skill') {
59	    const { findConditionalUnit } = await import('@/bootcamps/conditionals/content');
60	    const unit = findConditionalUnit(target.unitId);
61	    if (!unit) throw new Error('Choose an existing conditional skill.');
62	    label = unit.title;
63	    params.set('unit', unit.id);
64	  } else if (target.scope === 'area') {
65	    const area = CONDITIONAL_AREAS.find(option => option.id === target.area);
66	    if (!area) throw new Error('Choose an existing conditional area.');
67	    label = area.title;
68	    params.set('area', area.id);
69	  } else params.set('phase', 'conditional');
70	  const mode = target.scope === 'general' ? 'practice' : target.mode;
71	  const route = mode === 'practice' ? `${base}/practice?${params}` : target.scope === 'skill'
72	    ? `${base}/lessons/${encodeURIComponent(target.unitId!)}?${params}` : `${base}?${params}`;
73	  return {
74	    route, title: `${label}: ${mode === 'learn' ? 'learning and practice' : 'practice'}`,
75	    description: mode === 'learn' ? 'Teaching, guided activities and practice.' : 'Practice with explanations and optional hints.',
76	    target: { scope: target.scope, mode, difficulty: target.difficulty,
77	      ...(target.scope === 'skill' ? { unitId: target.unitId } : {}), ...(target.scope === 'area' ? { area: target.area } : {}) } as ConditionalAssignmentTarget,
78	    key: conditionalTargetKey({ ...target, mode }),
79	  };
80	}
81	
82	/** Normalize only exact former generated copy; teacher-written wording stays intact. */
83	export function conditionalAssignmentDisplayCopy(
84	  target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget,
85	  generated: { title: string; description: string },
86	  stored: { title?: string | null; description?: string | null },
87	) {
88	  let previousTitle: string | undefined;
89	  let previousDescription: string | undefined;
90	  if (['skill', 'area', 'general'].includes(target.scope)) {
91	    const mode = target.scope === 'general' ? 'practice' : target.mode;
92	    const suffix = mode === 'learn' ? 'learning and practice' : 'practice';
93	    const label = generated.title.slice(0, -(`: ${suffix}`.length));
94	    const level = `Level ${['beginner', 'medium', 'advanced'].indexOf(target.difficulty) + 1}`;
95	    previousTitle = `${label}: ${level} ${suffix}`;
96	    previousDescription = mode === 'learn'
97	      ? `Teaching, guided activities and assigned ${level.toLowerCase()} practice.`
98	      : `Assigned ${level.toLowerCase()} practice with explanations and optional hints.`;
99	  }
100	  return {
101	    title: !stored.title || stored.title === previousTitle ? generated.title : stored.title,
102	    description: !stored.description || stored.description === previousDescription ? generated.description : stored.description,
103	  };
104	}

2784	+  return !!run.drafts[questionId]?.checked || attempts.some(attempt => attempt.questionId === questionId && attempt.id.startsWith(`${run.id}:`));
2785	+}
2786	+export function nextUncheckedQuestionIndex(run: ConditionalRun, attempts: ConditionalAttempt[], after = -1): number {
2787	+  const pending = (id: string) => !hasCheckedQuestion(run, id, attempts);
2788	+  const next = run.questionIds.findIndex((id, index) => index > after && pending(id));
2789	+  return next >= 0 ? next : run.questionIds.findIndex(pending);
2790	+}
2791	+export function reconcileConditionalRun(run: ConditionalRun, questions: { id: string; unitId: string; difficulty: ConditionalDifficulty }[], attempts: ConditionalAttempt[]): ConditionalRun {
2792	+  const savedIds = new Set(run.questionIds);
2793	+  const removedIds = new Set<string>();
2794	+  const addedIds: string[] = [];
2795	+  for (const revision of openingQuestionRevisions) {
2796	+    if (![...revision.previousIds, ...revision.addedIds].some(id => savedIds.has(id))) continue;
2797	+    const allowedIds = new Set(questions.filter(question => question.unitId === revision.unitId && question.difficulty === revision.difficulty).map(question => question.id));
2798	+    for (const [retiredId, replacementId] of Object.entries(revision.replacements)) {
2799	+      if (savedIds.has(retiredId) && allowedIds.has(replacementId)) removedIds.add(retiredId);
2800	+    }
2801	+    for (const id of revision.addedIds) if (allowedIds.has(id) && !savedIds.has(id)) addedIds.push(id);
2802	+  }
2803	+  if (!removedIds.size && !addedIds.length) return run;
2804	+  const questionIds = [...run.questionIds.filter(id => !removedIds.has(id)), ...addedIds];
2805	+  const currentId = run.questionIds[run.index];
2806	+  const currentIndex = questionIds.indexOf(currentId);
2807	+  const keepCurrent = !run.finished && currentIndex >= 0 && questions.some(question => question.id === currentId);
2808	+  const updated = { ...run, questionIds };
2809	+  const pendingIndex = nextUncheckedQuestionIndex(updated, attempts);
2810	+  const index = keepCurrent ? currentIndex : pendingIndex >= 0 ? pendingIndex : questionIds.length;
2811	+  return { ...updated, index, finished: !keepCurrent && pendingIndex < 0 };
2812	+}

2815	 }

'Your arrow preserves the relationship.' : 'Your arrow reverses this relationship.'}</strong><p>{item.why}</p><p>{item.readback}</p><button onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % items.length); setLeft(null); setChecked(false); }}>{index === items.length - 1 ? 'Practice these again' : 'Next sentence'}</button></div>}</div></div>;
138	}
139	type IndicatorExplanation = {
140	  title: string;
141	  paragraphs: string[];
142	  prompt: string;
143	  reasoning: string[];
144	  after?: string[];
145	};

222	      ],
223	    },
224	    {

226	      paragraphs: [

228	        "A fee is paid by any applicant who submits late moves the target later in the sentence. It still tells us what follows for late-submitting applicants. Locate what any refers to rather than using sentence position as a placement instruction.",

248	      ],

Only members are admitted points to members, the necessary target. Different grammatical targets; the same complete relationship.",
251	      ],
252	    },
253	    {
254	      title: 'Only if: a condition that must be met',
255	      paragraphs: [
256	        "The student needs to complete the project for graduation. Only if makes project completion a requirement; it doesn't make completion a promise of graduation.",
257	        "The target is the complete condition completes the project. If graduation occurs, that condition has to be met. That is why it belongs on the required side.",
258	      ],
259	      prompt: 'A student completed the project. Could the student still fail to graduate without contradicting this sentence?',
260	      reasoning: [

262	        "Graduates → Completes project preserves what graduation requires. Reversing that arrow would turn the requirement into a guarantee.",
263	      ],
264	      after: [
265	        "Watch what changes when only is added to if below. One sentence makes a pass enough for entry. The other requires a pass for entry. Compare what each one lets you conclude from having a pass.",
266	      ],
267	    },
268	    {
269	      title: 'Only when: an occasion that is required',
270	      paragraphs: [
271	        "The sentence restricts reviews to occasions with a filed complaint. Filing a complaint is necessary for a review under this rule. It isn't stated enough to produce one.",
272	        "Only when a complaint is filed does a review occur moves the required condition to the front. It keeps the same relationship. Find what the sentence requires rather than assuming the first condition goes left.",
273	      ],
274	      prompt: 'A complaint has been filed. Does that alone establish that a review occurs?',
275	      reasoning: [
276	        "No. A filed complaint with no review fits the sentence. The statement restricts reviews to occasions with a filed complaint; it doesn't say every complaint produces one.",
277	        "Review occurs → Complaint filed keeps the required occasion on the right. If we learn that a review occurs, this rule lets us conclude a complaint was filed.",
278	      ],
279	    },

282	      paragraphs: [
283	        "The service needs a station wherever it operates. Only where points to station availability as the requirement.",
284	        "Compare the Group 1 sentence: Where a station is available, the service operates. That made availability enough. This sentence requires availability for operation. Adding only changes the direction even though both sentences mention the same ideas.",
285	      ],
286	      prompt: 'The service operates at South Square. What must be true there?',
287	      reasoning: [
288	        "A station is available there. Operation at South Square meets the condition the rule tells us about, so availability is required at that same location.",
289	        "A station somewhere else wouldn't satisfy this requirement. Read the diagram as: if the service operates at a location, a station is available there.",
290	      ],
291	    },
292	    {
293	      title: 'Always: identify what holds in every covered case',
294	      paragraphs: [
295	        "Read the subject before deciding what always does. This sentence tells us that receiving an invitation holds in every case of someone being a finalist.",
296	        "That makes an invitation necessary for finalist status under this rule. Always modifies receives an invitation. A familiar word helps only after we understand its job in the whole sentence.",

299	      reasoning: [

301	        "Always hasn't told us that invitations go only to finalists. The invitation belongs on the right as the result each finalist must receive; starting from it would reverse the relationship.",
302	      ],
303	    },
304	    {
305	      title: 'Requires: the target can come before the word',
306	      paragraphs: [
307	        "What does entry require? A current pass. That complete idea is necessary. A pass that has expired doesn't meet the condition the sentence gives us.",
308	        "Now move the requirement: A current pass is required for entry. The pass comes before required, but its role hasn't changed. Automatically taking the words after the indicator would point you toward entry and reverse the rule.",
309	      ],

314	      ],

320	        "The sentence hasn't supplied a fact proving that every finalist complied. Keep required to in the complete target so that your diagram preserves the difference between an obligation and a completed action.",
321	      ],

326	      ],

334	function IndicatorSections({ id }: { id: string }) {
335	  return <>{indicatorExplanations[id].map((lesson, index) => {
336	    const item = examples[id][index];
337	    return <Section key={item.word} title={lesson.title} expressionNumber={index + 1}>
338	      <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote>
339	      {lesson.paragraphs.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}
340	      <ThinkPause prompt={lesson.prompt}>
341	        {lesson.reasoning.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}
342	        <Arrow left={item.left} right={item.right}/>
343	        <p className="co-readback">{item.readback}</p>
344	      </ThinkPause>
345	      {lesson.after?.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}
346	      {id === 'group-2' && item.word === 'only if' && <DirectionSwitch/>}
347	    </Section>;
348	  })}</>;
349	}
350	
351	function Fundamentals({ roles = false }: { roles?: boolean }) {
352	  return <>
353	    {!roles && <>
354	      <Section title="One condition tells you something else">
355	        <p>A <strong>condition</strong> is something that can be true or false of the situation we're considering. A door is open. A person has a ticket. An order has been packed. Each gives us an idea whose truth we can ask about.</p>
356	        <p>A <strong>conditional statement</strong> connects two conditions. It tells us that if one is true, the other has to be true too. It doesn't tell us that the first condition actually occurred. It tells us what would follow if it did.</p>
357	        <p>That distinction matters. You can understand a rule about people who have tickets without knowing whether anyone has a ticket. Later, a fact about one person may give you a way to use the rule.</p>
358	        <div className="co-intro-relationship"><div><span>One condition is met</span><strong>If this is true...</strong></div><div><span>The statement guarantees another</span><strong>...this has to be true too.</strong></div></div>
359	        <p>You don't need symbols yet. First, understand what the sentence is saying.</p>
360	      </Section>
361	      <Section title="Why learn this?" dark>
362	        <p>On the LSAT, you're often deciding whether a conclusion follows from what you've been told. A conditional may supply the connection you need. It can also show you exactly where an argument asks you to accept more than its information supports.</p>
363	        <p>The important part is the direction. If one condition guarantees another, can we start with the other condition and go back? Another statement might allow that. But the first rule alone may not.</p>
364	        <p>We'll work with one everyday rule and change what we know about a particular order. You'll see a conclusion the rule supports and another that would need more information. That distinction helps you check what must be true and decide whether an argument has gone too far.</p>
365	      </Section>
366	      <Section title="A guarantee is stronger than a tendency">
367	        <p>Compare these two statements:</p>
368	        <div className="co-intro-relationship"><div><span>A tendency</span><strong>Orders marked ready are usually packed.</strong><p>A ready order could be an exception.</p></div><div><span>A conditional rule</span><strong>Every order marked ready has been packed.</strong><p>A ready order cannot be an exception under this rule.</p></div></div>
369	        <p>Usually leaves room for exceptions. You could find an order marked ready that hasn't been packed and still accept the first statement. The second doesn't leave that possibility open. Every order with that label has been packed.</p>
370	        <p>For an LSAT question, take the stated rule as given. A real shop could make a mistake, but we're asking what follows from this particular statement. The rule gives us a guarantee within the information we're considering.</p>
371	      </Section>
372	      <Section title="Read one rule in ordinary English">
373	        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>
374	        <p>There are two complete ideas here: an order is <strong>marked ready</strong>, and an order <strong>has been packed</strong>. The sentence tells us something about every order that meets the first condition.</p>
375	        <p>Read it this way: <strong>if an order is marked ready, then it has been packed.</strong> We don't need to know why the shop uses that label. We need to know what the sentence tells us about an order that has it.</p>
376	        <p>The rule itself doesn't identify a particular ready order. A separate fact can tell us whether one of the conditions is met in a particular case.</p>
377	        <ThinkPause prompt="Order A is marked ready. What do we now know about it?">
378	          <p><strong>Order A has been packed.</strong> The rule covers every order marked ready, and the fact tells us that Order A is one of those orders. That's the connection. The rule tells us what has to be true of the group; the fact lets us apply it to this case.</p>
379	          <p>We haven't learned that every order in the shop is packed. Our conclusion concerns Order A because that's the order the fact tells us about.</p>
380	        </ThinkPause>
381	      </Section>
382	      <Section title="The arrow is a shorter way to write it">
383	        <p>Now that we understand the sentence, we can give the two conditions shorter labels. The arrow records the connection between them.</p>
384	        <Arrow left="Marked ready" right="Packed"/>
385	        <p>Read it from left to right: <strong>if the order is marked ready, then it has been packed.</strong> The condition on the left gives us enough information to establish the condition on the right under this rule.</p>
386	        <p>The arrow doesn't mean “and then.” Packing could happen before the label is added. It also doesn't claim the label causes the packing. We're recording what follows logically, not a timeline.</p>
387	      </Section>
388	      <Section title="Change the fact, keep the rule">
389	        <p>Keep the rule exactly the same. This time, the fact tells us about packing instead of the ready label.</p>
390	        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>
391	        <ThinkPause prompt="Order B has been packed. Do we know whether it is marked ready?">
392	          <p><strong>We don't know yet.</strong> Order B could be marked ready. It could also be packed and waiting for a ready label. Both possibilities fit the statement.</p>
393	          <p>The rule tells us about every ready order. It hasn't told us that every packed order gets that label. To conclude that Order B is ready, we'd need that additional connection.</p>
394	        </ThinkPause>
395	        <p>In the drawing below, ready orders sit inside packed orders because every ready order is packed. A packed order can fit inside or outside the ready group. Use the two facts to compare what each one establishes.</p>
396	        <SetExplorer/>
397	      </Section>
398	      <Section title="Try the same reasoning with a new rule">
399	        <p>You've used the rule in its stated direction and checked what happens when you start from its result. Try that distinction with a different sentence.</p>
400	        <Check prompt="Every volunteer wears a badge. Ari wears a badge. Is Ari necessarily a volunteer?" options={['Yes, the badge proves it.', 'No, Ari could have a badge for another reason.']} correct={1} feedback={['You used the rule backward. Every volunteer has a badge, but the sentence does not say everyone with a badge is a volunteer. A staff member wearing one would fit the rule too.', 'A badge alone leaves volunteer status unknown. The sentence allows a staff member with a badge; it has not restricted badges to volunteers.']}/>
401	        <p>A matching result can make the reverse feel plausible. Check whether the sentence actually gave you that direction before accepting it.</p>
402	        <p>We can now name the two jobs the conditions are doing. One is enough to establish the other. The other is required whenever the first holds.</p>
403	      </Section>
404	    </>}
405	    {roles && <>
406	      <Section title="Sufficient means enough">
407	        <p>An order being marked ready is sufficient for it being packed. It's enough. Once we know the order is marked ready, this rule gives us what we need to conclude it has been packed.</p>
408	        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>
409	        <p>You don't need a second fact about its contents. The rule already tells you what must be true of every order with that label. We call being marked ready the <strong>sufficient condition</strong>. Here, sufficient means enough to establish packing under this particular rule.</p>
410	        <ThinkPause prompt="Order C is marked ready. Do you need another fact about C to know it has been packed?">
411	          <p>No. The ready label meets the condition the rule tells us about. The rule and that fact are enough to establish packing.</p>
412	          <p>This doesn't make the label the only possible way to find out that an order is packed. It is one condition that is enough under this rule.</p>
413	        </ThinkPause>
414	      </Section>
415	      <Section title="Enough does not mean the only way">
416	        <p>Suppose Order D isn't marked ready. We no longer have the fact that allowed us to use the rule in its stated direction. But that doesn't settle the packing question.</p>
417	        <ThinkPause prompt="Order D isn't marked ready. Could it still have been packed?">
418	          <p>Yes. D could be packed and waiting for its label. It could also be unpacked. Neither possibility creates a ready order that hasn't been packed, so both fit the rule.</p>
419	          <p>The sentence gives us one condition that settles packing. It hasn't said packing happens only for those orders. When that sufficient condition isn't present, the other condition may still be true.</p>
420	        </ThinkPause>
421	        <p>Use a new rule to check whether you can recognize what is enough.</p>
422	        <Check prompt="Every delivery marked collected has a pickup receipt. What is sufficient under this rule?" options={['Marked collected', 'Has a pickup receipt']} correct={0} feedback={['Marked collected is enough to establish a pickup receipt. The rule tells us what is true of every delivery with that label.', 'You chose the required result. Having a receipt is not stated enough for the collected label. The sentence gives us the direction from marked collected to receipt, not its reverse.']}/>
423	      </Section>
424	      <Section title="Necessary means required">
425	        <p>Now look at the other condition in the same rule. An order marked ready has to have been packed. Being packed is necessary for being marked ready under this rule.</p>
426	        <p>We call being packed the <strong>necessary condition</strong>. Necessary means required. It names something that has to be true whenever the sufficient condition is true.</p>
427	        <p>Ask “required for what?” Here, required for an order marked ready under this rule. The relationship gives the word its meaning.</p>
428	        <ThinkPause prompt="Which condition does a ready order have to meet under this rule?">
429	          <p>It has to be packed. Otherwise we'd have a ready order that wasn't packed, which the sentence rules out.</p>
430	          <p>The ready label is enough to establish packing, and packing is required for every ready order. Those are two views of the same relationship.</p>
431	        </ThinkPause>
432	        <div className="co-role-pair"><div><h3>Sufficient</h3><p>Marked ready: enough to establish packing.</p><strong>Goes left</strong></div><div><h3>Necessary</h3><p>Packed: required for every ready order.</p><strong>Goes right</strong></div></div>
433	        <p>Now compare what each starting fact tells us. Keep the same rule while you switch between the ready label and packing.</p>
434	        <GateExplorer/>
435	      </Section>
436	      <Section title="Having a requirement does not guarantee the result">
437	        <p>Let's use a different requirement.</p>
438	        <blockquote>Entering the archive requires a pass.</blockquote>
439	        <p>The sentence tells us what someone needs for entry: a pass. If someone enters the archive, they have that pass. That's why has a pass belongs on the required side.</p>
440	        <Arrow left="Enters the archive" right="Has a pass"/>
441	        <ThinkPause prompt="Mina has a pass. Does this sentence establish that she entered the archive?">
442	          <p>No. Mina could have a pass and remain outside. The rule tells us what entry requires; it doesn't tell us that every person with a pass enters.</p>
443	          <p>Necessary doesn't mean enough. A requirement can be present while the other condition remains unknown. To conclude Mina entered, we'd need information establishing that additional connection.</p>
444	        </ThinkPause>
445	        <Check prompt="Every finalist receives an invitation. What is necessary for being a finalist under this rule?" options={['Receiving an invitation', 'Being the winner']} correct={0} feedback={['The rule requires an invitation for each finalist. That makes receiving one necessary for being a finalist under this sentence.', 'You added a condition the sentence has not given us. It says every finalist receives an invitation, not that every finalist wins. The invitation is the requirement stated here.']}/>
446	      </Section>
447	      <Section title="Read the roles in the relationship">
448	        <p>In <strong>Marked ready → Packed</strong>, the left condition is sufficient for the right: it's enough to establish it. The right condition is necessary for the left: it's required whenever the left holds.</p>
449	        <p>These names describe roles in this rule. A different sentence could give the same idea a different role. Don't ask whether a pass is sufficient or necessary by itself. Ask what the complete sentence says having the pass does.</p>
450	        <p>The next two groups give us ways to recognize these roles in varied wording. We'll keep returning to the meaning because an indicator can only help if you know what complete idea it refers to.</p>
451	      </Section>
452	    </>}
453	  </>;
454	}
455	
456	function GroupLessons({ id }: { id: string }) {
457	  const group = GROUPS.find(item => item.id === (id === 'group-1' ? 'g1' : 'g2'))!;
458	  const first = id === 'group-1';
459	  return <>
460	    <Section title={first ? 'Find the sufficient idea' : 'Find what is required'}>
461	      <p>{first
462	        ? "You already know what a sufficient condition does. When it holds, the rule gives you the other condition. Group 1 helps you recognize that job in several kinds of sentence. We'll work through the meaning of each expression, then practice finding its complete target."
463	        : "Group 1 helped us find a condition that is enough. Group 2 turns our attention to what must be true when the other condition holds. A requirement belongs on the right because the left condition cannot hold under the rule without it. Having the requirement, though, may leave the left condition unknown."}</p>
464	      <div className="co-vocabulary">{group.words.map(word => <span key={word}>{word}</span>)}</div>
465	      <p>An <strong>indicator</strong> is an expression that helps us recognize the relationship. Its <strong>target</strong> is the complete idea it refers to. Finding that idea matters more than taking the next few words in the sentence.</p>
466	    </Section>
467	    <IndicatorSections id={id}/>
468	    <Section title={first ? 'Find the target, then place it' : 'Find the requirement, then place it'}>
469	      <p>{first
470	        ? "Add what to the indicator when that gives you a sensible question. If what? All what? The only what? Your answer should be the whole idea the expression refers to. Then check whether that idea really is enough to establish the other condition."
471	        : "Ask what the indicator refers to. Only what? Only if what? What is required? The target can come before or after the expression, so check its meaning in the complete sentence."}</p>
472	      <p>Work through the expressions below. First choose the complete target. Then place it on its side of the arrow and read the relationship in ordinary English.</p>
473	      <TranslationLab id={id}/>
474	    </Section>
475	    <Section title="Keep the whole condition" dark>

478	        ? "Applicant alone isn't the condition. The sentence is about an applicant who submits late. Keep that restriction when you shorten the idea; otherwise your diagram would make a claim about on-time applicants too."
479	        : "A current pass is required. Has a pass drops current and would include an expired pass. Shortening a label should save writing while preserving what the sentence requires."}</p>

481	        ? ['You dropped the late-submission restriction. Applicant alone would cover every applicant, including someone who submits on time. 482	        : ['This keeps the full requirement. Entry needs a current pass under the rule; an expired pass does not meet it.', 'You dropped current from the requirement. Has any pass would include an expired pass, which is not what the sentence requires.']}/>
483	    </Section>
484	    <Section title={first ? 'Read the only as a complete expression' : 'Compare only with the only'}>
485	      {first ? <>
486	        <blockquote>The only people admitted are members.</blockquote>
487	        <p>The only what? The only people admitted. The complete target is admitted people. If someone is admitted, the sentence tells us they're a member.</p>
488	        <Arrow left="Admitted" right="Member"/>
489	        <p>A member who stays outside fits this rule. Membership hasn't been made enough for admission. Check that your diagram keeps that possibility open.</p>

491	      </> : <>
492	        <div className="co-word-comparison"><div><blockquote>Only members are admitted.</blockquote><p>Only refers to members. Membership is required for admission, so member goes right.</p></div><div><blockquote>The only people admitted are members.</blockquote><p>The only refers to admitted people. Admission is enough to establish membership, so admitted goes left.</p></div></div>
493	        <Arrow left="Admitted" right="Member"/>
494	        <p>Both sentences express this relationship. The expressions help us locate different targets, but the complete meaning determines the same direction.</p>

496	      </>}
497	    </Section>
498	    <Section title="From sentence to diagram">
499	      <p>You have seen the expressions with help. Now use a new sentence. Identify both complete ideas, choose the sufficient one for the left, and check whether your diagram says what the sentence says.</p>
500	      <p>If you know the meaning but hesitate over the arrow, imagine the left condition is true. Does the sentence then require the right condition? Read the whole relationship in English. If it makes a promise the original didn't make, change the direction.</p>
501	      <DiagramBuilder id={id}/>
502	      <details className="co-practical-note"><summary>Why write a diagram when I understand the sentence?</summary><p>For one short sentence, you may be able to hold the connection in your head. Here, writing it makes your direction and complete conditions visible so you can check them. If your first diagram doesn't match the meaning, find the exact phrase you misread and revise that part.</p></details>
503	    </Section>
504	    <Section title="Say the rule without the scaffold">
505	      <p>Read the new sentence and explain its direction before revealing. If you hesitate, ask what the indicator refers to, then try the proposed relationship in ordinary English. Compare your explanation with the one that appears.</p>
506	      <Flashcards id={id}/>
507	    </Section>
508	    <Section title="Check the meaning, not just the word">
509	      <Check prompt={first ? 'A report is reviewed if submitted before noon. Which is the same rule?' : 'A student graduates only if the project is completed. Which is the same rule?'} options={first ? ['If reviewed, it was submitted before noon.', 'If submitted before noon, it is reviewed.'] : ['If the student graduates, the project was completed.', 'If the project is completed, the student graduates.']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} feedback={first
510	        ? ['You reversed the if relationship. Submission before noon is enough for review; the sentence has not said review proves when the report was submitted.', 'This preserves the if-clause as the sufficient condition. Submission before noon gives us review even though it appears second in the original sentence.']
511	        : ['This preserves project completion as a requirement for graduation. It does not turn completing the project into a promise of graduation.', 'You turned the only-if requirement into a guarantee. Graduation requires completion, but completion alone is not stated enough to graduate.']}/>
512	      <p>The guided practice asks you to write your reasoning with the method beside you. Then the attached drill gives you fresh questions. Use the explanation to inspect the direction and complete conditions, not only whether you chose the same answer.</p>
513	    </Section>
514	  </>;

517	export function ConditionalOpeningLesson({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, continueLabel = 'Continue to guided practice', previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {
518	  const [motionPaused, setMotionPaused] = useState(false);
519	  const group = unit.id === 'group-1' || unit.id === 'group-2';
520	  const orientation = unit.id === 'orientation';
521	  const title = group ? unit.id === 'group-1' ? 'Group 1: what is enough?' : 'Group 2: what is required?' : orientation ? 'What are conditionals?' : 'What is enough, and what is required?';
522	  const subtitle = orientation
523	    ? "A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too. Let's see what that connection lets you conclude, and what it leaves open."
524	    : group ? unit.id === 'group-1'
525	      ? 'These expressions can point to the sufficient idea. Read what each one says before deciding which complete condition belongs on the left.'
526	      : 'These expressions can identify a requirement. Find the complete idea the sentence says you need, then check what its presence does and does not prove.'
527	    : "The same rule gives its two conditions different jobs. Let's take each one seriously before giving it a place in the diagram.";
528	  return <div className="conditional-note co-opening" data-motion-paused={motionPaused}>
529	    <DynamicIslandTOC key={unit.id} selector=".co-opening [data-toc]"/>
530	    <header className={'co-hero ' + (orientation ? 'co-intro-hero' : '')}>
531	      <p className="co-kicker">Conditional logic</p><h1>{title}</h1><p>{subtitle}</p>
532	      <a href="#co-reading">{orientation ? 'Start with the meaning' : 'Begin reading'} <span aria-hidden="true">↓</span></a>
533	      <button type="button" className="co-motion-toggle" aria-pressed={motionPaused} onClick={() => setMotionPaused(!motionPaused)}>{motionPaused ? 'Resume motion' : 'Pause motion'}</button>
534	      {!orientation && <div className="co-hero-orbit" aria-hidden="true"><span>Enough</span><i>→</i><span>Required</span></div>}
535	    </header>
536	    <article id="co-reading">
537	      {group ? <GroupLessons id={unit.id}/> : <Fundamentals roles={unit.id === 'sufficient-necessary'}/>}
538	      <ConditionalOpeningMedia unitId={unit.id}/>
539	      <footer className="co-footer">
540	        <p>{reviewed ? 'You marked this lesson as read. You can return to the examples whenever you need.' : 'Marking this lesson as read records review. Your practice gives you a chance to use the reasoning yourself.'}</p>
541	        <div className="co-choices">{previousHref && <Link to={previousHref}>Previous lesson</Link>}<button type="button" onClick={onComplete}>{reviewed ? 'Lesson read' : 'Mark lesson as read'}</button><Link className="co-primary" to={continueHref}>{continueLabel} <span aria-hidden="true">→</span></Link></div>
542	      </footer>
543	    </article>
544	  </div>;
545	}

33	function Recall({ items }: { items: Card[] }) {

36	  const answerId = useId();
37	  const item = items[index];
38	  return <div className="co-flash">
39	    <p className="co-kicker">Review · {index + 1} of {items.length}</p>
40	    <div className={`co-flash-face ${revealed ? 'co-flipped' : ''}`}>
41	      <strong>{item.question}</strong><span>Answer in your own words, then compare.</span>
42	      <div id={answerId} aria-live="polite">{revealed && <p>{item.answer}</p>}</div>
43	    </div>
44	    <div className="co-choices">
45	      <button type="button" aria-expanded={revealed} aria-controls={answerId} onClick={() => setRevealed(!revealed)}>{revealed ? 'Hide recall explanation' : 'Reveal recall explanation'}</button>
46	      <button type="button" disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => { setIndex(index - 1); setRevealed(false); }}>Previous example</button>
47	      <button type="button" onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % items.length); setRevealed(false); }}>{index === items.length - 1 ? 'Start these again' : 'Next example'}</button>
48	    </div>
49	  </div>;
50	}

Is permission the same as making or receiving a request?", ["Imani is an authorized visitor, so the rule gives Imani permission to request a copy.", "It doesn't say Imani uses that permission or receives a copy. May request is the conclusion the wording supports."]),
66	        question("orientation-a2", "Every sensor certified for underwater use has a sealed housing. Sensor K is certified for indoor use. What follows about its housing?", ["It is sealed.", "It is not sealed.", "Neither conclusion is established."], 2, "Does certified for indoor use meet certified for underwater use?", ["No. The rule requires the underwater certification before it promises a sealed housing.", "Indoor certification doesn't establish that condition. K could have a sealed housing or lack one without contradicting what we've been told."]),
67	        question("orientation-a3", "The manual says: If the warning light flashes, shut down the machine. The light flashes. What follows from this instruction alone?", ["The machine has shut down.", "The instruction calls for shutdown.", "The machine cannot shut down."], 1, "The light flashes. What response does the instruction require, and what action has actually been reported?", ["The flashing light meets the condition for the shutdown instruction.", "The instruction therefore calls for shutdown. It doesn't report that anyone followed it or that the machine has already shut down."]),
68	      ],

71	  {
72	    id: 'rules-facts', title: 'Facts, Rules, and What Follows', area: 'understanding', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ['group-2'],
73	    goal: 'Apply rules to case facts and distinguish case conclusions from new rules.',
74	    teaching: [
75	      'A fact tells you something about a case. A rule connects conditions. Every digitized map is searchable is a rule. Map R is digitized is a fact. Match the fact to the rule\'s starting condition to conclude that Map R is searchable.',
76	      'For digitized → searchable, digitized establishes searchable. Not searchable rules out digitized: a digitized map would have to be searchable. Searchable alone leaves digitization unknown. Not digitized alone leaves searchability unknown.',
77	      'Two rules can create a new rule. Digitized → searchable and searchable → indexed give digitized → indexed. They do not establish that an indexed map exists. Add a digitized case as a separate fact to establish an indexed case.',
78	      'Make the reasoning visible: Fact: Map R is digitized. Rule: every digitized map is searchable. Match: the fact meets the complete digitized-map condition for R. Conclusion: R is searchable. Why: the rule covers that same map. Contrast this with two rules alone: digitized → searchable → indexed proves a new rule, without proving a fact about R.',
79	      'Keep one rule unchanged and vary only the fact: translated notice → archived notice. Notice H is translated: H is archived. H is not archived: H is not translated. H is archived: translation remains unknown. H is not translated: archiving remains unknown. In the last two cases, give an allowed example rather than forcing a conclusion.',
80	      'Requires approval does not mean has received approval. A late submission may require approval while it is still waiting for review. If the next rule begins with received approval, the requirement does not activate it. Preserve the whole condition before comparing the fact and rule.',
81	    ],
82	    method: ['List rules separately from case facts.', 'Keep the same subject and exact predicates when matching.', 'Use a present trigger or an absent required result.', 'Label the output as a case conclusion or a conditional rule.'],
83	    workedExample: { statements: ['Every waterproof notebook has coated pages.', 'Notebook A does not have coated pages.'], reasoning: ['Waterproof status would require coated pages.', 'That required result is absent for A.'], conclusion: 'Notebook A is not waterproof.' },
84	    guidedExample: question('rules-facts-guide', 'Every translated notice is archived. Notice H is archived. Before choosing, identify the fact, rule, and match. State your conclusion and explain why it follows or remains open. What follows about translation?', ['H is translated.', 'H is not translated.', 'Translation is unknown.'], 2, 'Archiving is the required result, not the trigger. An untranslated archived notice would fit the rule.', ['Translated notices must be archived.', 'Other notices may also be archived, so the fact settles neither translation nor its absence.']),
85	    commonMistake: { mistake: 'Calling an allowed case a proven case.', repair: 'Ask whether every situation fitting the information includes the conclusion. If both outcomes fit, say unknown.' },
86	    drills: {
87	      beginner: [
88	        question('rules-facts-b1', 'Every tested battery has a sticker. Battery P was tested. What follows?', ['P has a sticker.', 'P is the only tested battery.', 'All stickered batteries were tested.'], 0, 'Apply the rule to P without adding a reverse rule.', ['P meets the tested condition.', 'The sticker follows for P alone.']),
89	        question('rules-facts-b2', 'Every polished lens is clear. Lens J is not clear. What follows?', ['J is polished.', 'J is not polished.', 'J is clear after all.'], 1, 'Could a polished lens lack the required clarity?', ['Polishing would require clarity.', 'J lacks clarity, so J cannot be polished under the rule.']),
90	        question('rules-facts-b3', 'Every reserved seat has a marker. Seat W is not reserved. Its marker status is:', ['Guaranteed present.', 'Guaranteed absent.', 'Unknown.'], 2, 'The rule speaks only about reserved seats.', ['An unreserved seat does not activate the rule.', 'A marker may be present or absent without breaking it.']),
91	      ],
92	      medium: [
93	        question('rules-facts-m1', 'Every encrypted file is restricted. Every restricted file is logged. No case fact is supplied. Which follows?', ['Some logged files exist.', 'Every encrypted file is logged.', 'Every logged file is encrypted.'], 1, 'Match the shared restricted condition.', ['Encryption leads to restriction, which leads to logging.', 'The resulting rule does not assert a file exists.']),
94	        question('rules-facts-m2', 'Every approved replacement part is stocked. Part D is a replacement part, but approval is unknown. What follows?', ['D is stocked.', 'D is not stocked.', 'Stocking remains unknown.'], 2, 'Replacement part and approved replacement part differ.', ['The trigger includes approval.', 'The supplied fact does not meet that entire trigger.']),
95	        question('rules-facts-m3', 'Every public hearing is recorded. Hearing N is recorded. A student concludes it is public. Which repair is accurate?', ['Recorded → public was never given.', 'Public hearings cannot be recorded.', 'The rule has no starting condition.'], 0, 'Identify the direction the student added.', ['The rule runs from public status to recording.', 'The student used its unsupported reverse.']),
96	      ],
97	      advanced: [
98	        question('rules-facts-a1', 'Every eligible applicant may appeal. Tavi is an eligible applicant. Which follows?', ['Tavi has appealed.', 'Tavi may appeal.', 'Tavi\'s appeal was accepted.'], 1, 'Preserve the modal word may.', ['Tavi satisfies the complete eligible-applicant condition, which grants appeal permission.', 'Permission establishes neither action nor acceptance.']),
99	        question('rules-facts-a2', 'Every licensed guide must carry a radio. Guide Z carries no radio. What follows about compliance?', ['Z cannot be licensed in real life.', 'If Z is licensed, Z violates the stated requirement.', 'Z must be unlicensed in real life.'], 1, 'A requirement can be violated; it is not a physical impossibility.', ['A licensed Z would be required to carry a radio.', 'The fact would show noncompliance, rather than proving absence of a license.']),
100	        question('rules-facts-a3', 'Every shortlisted design is displayed. Every displayed design is catalogued. Design E is not catalogued. What follows?', ['E is shortlisted.', 'E is not shortlisted.', 'No design is displayed.'], 1, 'Trace what shortlisting would force for E.', ['Shortlisting would lead through display to cataloguing.', 'E lacks the final required result, so E is not shortlisted.']),
101	      ],
