104	  {
105	    id: 'sufficient-necessary', title: "Enough to Guarantee, Required to Follow", area: 'understanding', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ["orientation"],
106	    goal: "Explain sufficient as enough and necessary as required, then check which cases fit the same rule.",
107	    teaching: [
108	      "Return to our rule: Every order marked ready has been packed. If you know an order is marked ready, that's enough to know it has been packed. You don't need another fact about that order. Being marked ready is the sufficient condition. Sufficient means enough, here enough to establish packing under this rule.",
109	      "Packed is the necessary condition. Necessary means required. A ready order has to have been packed, because an unpacked ready order would be an exception to our rule. So the same relationship has two sides: marked ready is enough to establish packed, and packed is required for marked ready.",
110	      "Does required mean enough? Suppose an order is packed. Must it be marked ready? We still don't know. Packing meets a requirement for ready status, but the rule hasn't said it settles that status. A packed order waiting for a ready label would fit the rule.",
111	      "Now suppose an order isn't marked ready. Does that settle whether it's packed? No. Our rule tells us what ready orders must have. An order without that label could be packed or unpacked. We haven't been given a fact that decides between them.",
112	      "Compare all four combinations under this one rule. Ready and packed fits. Not ready and packed fits too. Not ready and not packed also fits. Ready and not packed breaks the guarantee. A case fitting the rule means it is possible under that rule, not that the rule tells us it occurred.",
113	      "The names belong to this relationship. Packed is necessary for ready status here. In a different rule, such as every packed order has a tracking number, packed would be sufficient for that tracking number. Ask enough for what, or required for what, rather than giving an idea one permanent label.",
114	      "When a sentence feels unclear, try a Personalized If/Then. Read it naturally first. Then imagine you meet the condition you think is sufficient. What does the sentence force you to know? Check that thought against the whole sentence, not just against a feeling that the arrow looks right.",
115	      "A recovery code is required for a verified account. If my account is verified, what must it have? A recovery code. Now try the other direction: if I have a recovery code, must my account be verified? The sentence hasn't promised that. This check helps us hear why the requirement belongs on the right.",
116	    ],
117	    method: [
118	      "Read the whole sentence in ordinary English before deciding which condition is enough.",
119	      "Ask enough for what. Name both ideas completely: marked ready is enough to establish packed under our rule.",
120	      "Ask required for what. Packed is required for marked ready; that doesn't make packed enough to establish ready status.",
121	      "Check your interpretation with a Personalized If/Then. Try the proposed starting condition, then compare the reverse with the original sentence.",
122	      "Test a possible case against the actual guarantee. Keep a case that fits separate from a case the information establishes.",
123	    ],
124	    workedExample: {
125	      statements: ["Every order marked ready has been packed.", "Order C has been packed but is not marked ready."],
126	      reasoning: ["Packing is required for ready status. The rule hasn't promised ready status for every packed order.", "C can therefore be packed without a ready label. That case fits the rule; it doesn't supply a reverse rule."],
127	      conclusion: "Packed without marked ready fits this rule.",
128	    },
129	    guidedExample: question("sufficient-necessary-guide", "Every verified account has a recovery code. Before choosing, use a Personalized If/Then: imagine that your account is verified and state what you must know about it. Which combination violates the rule?", ["Verified, with a recovery code.", "Verified, without a recovery code.", "Unverified, with a recovery code."], 1, "If my account is verified, what does the rule require it to have? Compare the three cases with that promise.", ["A verified account has to have a recovery code under this rule.", "Verified without a code breaks that guarantee. A verified account with one fits, and an unverified account with one doesn't break any promise the rule made."]),
130	    commonMistake: { mistake: "Taking a required condition as enough to establish the other condition.", repair: "Try the reverse in English: if an order is packed, it must be marked ready. Did the sentence promise that? It didn't. Keep the requirement, but don't add that guarantee." },
131	    drills: {
132	      beginner: [
133	        question("sufficient-necessary-b1", "If a cabinet is fire-rated, it has a steel shell. Which condition does this rule identify as guaranteeing a steel shell?", ["Being fire-rated.", "Having a steel shell.", "Not being fire-rated."], 0, "If the cabinet is fire-rated, what do you know about its shell?", ["The rule makes fire-rated status enough to establish a steel shell.", "Having a steel shell is the promised result. The sentence hasn't identified that result or absence of fire-rating as its sufficient starting condition."]),
134	        question("sufficient-necessary-b2", "Every accredited course has an assessment. What is necessary for accreditation?", ["Absence of an assessment.", "Having an assessment.", "Being an evening course."], 1, "Imagine an accredited course. What does the rule say it has to have?", ["It has to have an assessment. An accredited course without one would break the rule.", "So an assessment is necessary for accreditation. The rule hasn't made every assessed course accredited."]),
135	        question("sufficient-necessary-b3", "Every packaged tube has a cap. Which case fits the rule?", ["Packaged, no cap.", "Not packaged, with a cap.", "Packaged, no cap, but small."], 1, "Which case avoids a packaged tube without a cap?", ["A tube that isn't packaged can have a cap without breaking the rule. The sentence only promises caps for packaged tubes.", "Both uncapped packaged cases break that promise. Being small doesn't excuse the missing cap."]),
136	      ],
137	      medium: [
138	        question("sufficient-necessary-m1", "For polished → reflective and reflective → visible, reflective is:", ["Necessary in both rules.", "Necessary in the first; sufficient in the second.", "Sufficient in both rules."], 1, "Read the two arrows separately. What role does reflective have in each relationship?", ["In polished → reflective, reflective is what polishing requires. It is necessary in that rule.", "In reflective → visible, reflective is enough to establish visible. It is sufficient there. The role changes with the relationship."]),
139	        question("sufficient-necessary-m2", "Every tagged specimen is stored. Specimen F is stored. Tagged status is:", ["Guaranteed.", "Ruled out.", "Unknown."], 2, "Could a specimen be stored without being tagged and still fit the sentence?", ["Yes. Tagged specimens must be stored, but the sentence doesn't restrict storage to tagged specimens.", "F could be tagged or untagged. Storage alone doesn't settle which, so tagged status is unknown."]),
140	        question("sufficient-necessary-m3", "If a seat is upgraded, it has extra legroom. A seat is not upgraded and has no extra legroom. This case is:", ["Forbidden.", "Allowed, but not established by the rule alone.", "Required for every seat."], 1, "What promise would an upgraded seat without extra legroom break? Does this case have an upgraded seat?", ["The guarantee applies to upgraded seats. This seat isn't upgraded, so its lack of extra legroom doesn't break that promise.", "The case is allowed. The rule by itself wouldn't tell us this case occurred; that is a separate claim in the question."]),
141	      ],
142	      advanced: [
143	        question("sufficient-necessary-a1", "Every paper accepted for the student symposium has a faculty sponsor. Which is sufficient for sponsorship under this rule?", ["Acceptance for any conference.", "Acceptance for the student symposium.", "Merely being a paper."], 1, "Which exact acceptance does the rule connect to having a faculty sponsor?", ["Acceptance for the student symposium is enough for a faculty sponsor under this rule.", "Acceptance for a different conference, or simply being a paper, doesn't meet that full sufficient idea."]),
144	        question("sufficient-necessary-a2", "Every authenticated token is valid. A token is valid but not authenticated. What does that show?", ["The rule is false.", "Validity can hold without authentication consistently with the rule.", "Authentication is necessary for validity."], 1, "Would a valid token without authentication break a promise about authenticated tokens?", ["No. The rule promises validity for authenticated tokens; it hasn't said every valid token is authenticated.", "A valid unauthenticated token therefore fits the relationship. It doesn't make authentication necessary for validity."]),
145	        question("sufficient-necessary-a3", "Every priority packet is routed through Server C. No priority packets are known to exist. Which is accurate?", ["Routing through C is necessary for priority status; no such packet is established.", "A packet through C must exist.", "Routing through C is sufficient for priority status."], 0, "Name the requirement for a priority packet, then ask whether the sentence identifies one.", ["A priority packet has to be routed through Server C, so that routing is necessary for priority status.", "The rule hasn't identified a priority packet. It establishes a requirement, not the existence of a packet meeting it or the reverse direction."]),
146	      ],
147	    },
148	  },
149	  {
150	    id: 'truth-validity', title: 'Does the Conclusion Have to Follow?', area: 'understanding', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ['rules-facts'],
151	    goal: 'Test whether true premises could coexist with a false conclusion.',
152	    teaching: [
153	      'Truth concerns whether a statement matches the facts. Validity concerns the connection between premises and conclusion. To test a deduction, temporarily accept its premises. Ask whether its conclusion could still be false.',
154	      'Every copper key is magnetic. This key is copper. Therefore this key is magnetic. The reasoning follows the given rule, even if the first premise is false outside the exercise. We are testing the inference, not endorsing the premise.',
155	      'A counterexample keeps every premise true while making the conclusion false. For A → B and B, therefore A, a case with B but no A does that. Invalid does not mean the conclusion must be false. It means the premises do not establish it.',
156	    ],
157	    method: ['Accept the supplied premises for the inference test.', 'Identify the exact conclusion.', 'Try to keep all premises true while making that conclusion false.', 'If such a case is possible, the conclusion is not guaranteed.'],
158	    workedExample: { statements: ['Every circular tile is blue.', 'Tile P is blue.', 'Claim: Tile P is circular.'], reasoning: ['The rule allows a blue square tile.', 'That case preserves the premises and defeats the claim.'], conclusion: 'Circularity is not guaranteed.' },
159	    guidedExample: question('truth-validity-guide', 'Every enrolled swimmer has a locker. Ren is enrolled as a swimmer. Therefore Ren has a locker. The inference is:', ['Valid from the premises.', 'Invalid because lockers may be expensive.', 'Invalid because enrollment is not necessary for a locker.'], 0, 'Only test the direction actually used.', ['Ren meets the rule\'s sufficient condition.', 'The conclusion is the required result, so it follows.']),
160	    commonMistake: { mistake: 'Rejecting an inference because a premise sounds unrealistic.', repair: 'First assess whether the conclusion follows if the premises are true. Assess premise truth as a separate question.' },
161	    drills: {
162	      beginner: [
163	        question('truth-validity-b1', 'Every gold leaf is transparent. Leaf R is gold. Therefore R is transparent. Does the conclusion follow from these premises?', ['Yes.', 'No, because gold is not normally transparent.', 'Only if transparent leaves are gold.'], 0, 'Treat the unusual premise as given.', ['R satisfies the gold condition.', 'Transparency follows by the stated rule regardless of real-world plausibility.']),
164	        question('truth-validity-b2', 'Every bound booklet has a cover. Booklet D has a cover. Therefore D is bound. The problem is:', ['The conclusion contradicts the rule.', 'The rule was reversed without support.', 'The premises establish no cover.'], 1, 'A cover may have another source.', ['The premises allow an unbound covered booklet.', 'They therefore do not guarantee binding.']),
165	        question('truth-validity-b3', 'An invalid argument\'s conclusion must be:', ['False.', 'True.', 'Neither; validity alone does not settle its truth.'], 2, 'Failure of proof is not proof of falsity.', ['Invalidity means the premises do not force the conclusion.', 'The conclusion may still be true for other reasons.']),
166	      ],
167	      medium: [
168	        question('truth-validity-m1', 'Every laminated card resists water. Card B resists water. Claim: B is laminated. Which counterexample works?', ['B is laminated and resists water.', 'B is not laminated and resists water.', 'B is laminated and does not resist water.'], 1, 'Keep the rule and the resistance fact true.', ['An unlaminated resistant card is permitted by the rule.', 'It makes the proposed conclusion false while preserving both premises.']),
169	        question('truth-validity-m2', 'Every active beacon transmits. Beacon H does not transmit. Therefore H is not active. This is:', ['Valid.', 'An unsupported reverse.', 'Invalid because it uses a negative fact.'], 0, 'Would activity be compatible with no transmission?', ['Activity would require transmission.', 'The absent required result excludes activity.']),
170	        question('truth-validity-m3', 'Every reserved bicycle has a lock. Bicycle Y is not reserved. Claim: Y has no lock. Which test defeats the claim?', ['Y is unreserved but locked.', 'Y is reserved but unlocked.', 'Y is reserved and locked.'], 0, 'Keep the explicit unreserved fact.', ['An unreserved locked bicycle violates no premise.', 'It shows that absence of reservation does not force absence of a lock.']),
171	      ],
172	      advanced: [
173	        question('truth-validity-a1', 'Every approved design is documented. Every documented design is archived. Design C is archived. Claim: C is approved. Which is accurate?', ['The chain proves approval.', 'An archived unapproved design is a counterexample.', 'Archiving contradicts approval.'], 1, 'The final result need not identify the initial trigger.', ['Both rules permit archiving designs that were never approved.', 'Such a design defeats the reverse conclusion.']),
174	        question('truth-validity-a2', 'Every enrolled adult in this workshop has a badge. Kira is an enrolled child with a badge. Claim: Kira is an adult. Which explains the failure?', ['The rule\'s adult condition cannot be inferred from a badge.', 'Children cannot have badges.', 'Workshop enrollment always establishes adulthood.'], 0, 'A modifier remains part of the sufficient condition.', ['The premise restricts what enrolled adults have.', 'It neither prohibits children\'s badges nor makes a badge proof of adulthood.']),
175	        question('truth-validity-a3', 'All encrypted backups are remote. No encrypted backup is known to exist. Claim: Some remote backup exists. Which possibility defeats the claim?', ['There are no backups.', 'There is an encrypted remote backup.', 'Every backup is encrypted.'], 0, 'A universal premise can hold with no qualifying cases.', ['With no backups, the universal rule has no counterexample.', 'The existential conclusion is nevertheless false.']),
176	      ],
177	    },
178	  },
179	  {
180	    id: 'negation', title: 'Say Exactly What Is Not True', area: 'understanding', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ['truth-validity'],
181	    goal: 'Negate a complete simple predicate without substituting an everyday opposite.',
182	    teaching: [
183	      'Negation denies the claim you actually have. The opposite of the room is warm is the room is not warm. The room is cold is stronger: a room can be neither warm nor cold.',
184	      'Keep the subject, time and scope. The panel is approved for outdoor use becomes the panel is not approved for outdoor use. It does not become the panel is not approved for any use.',
185	      'Negating a negative removes that negative: not not registered means registered. This lesson works with simple complete conditions. Negating AND/OR combinations and entire conditional rules requires the later lessons devoted to those structures.',
186	    ],
187	    method: ['Write the exact predicate being denied.', 'Add not to that predicate without changing its subject or scope.', 'Cancel a double negative when one is created.', 'Check that the claim and its negation cannot both be true or both be false.'],
188	    workedExample: { statements: ['The package arrived before noon.'], reasoning: ['Deny arrival before noon.', 'Arrival at noon, later arrival and nonarrival all fail the original claim.'], conclusion: 'The package did not arrive before noon.' },
189	    guidedExample: question('negation-guide', 'What is the exact negation of The sculpture is not insured?', ['The sculpture is expensive.', 'The sculpture is insured.', 'The sculpture is damaged.'], 1, 'Negate the existing not.', ['Denying not insured cancels that negative.', 'The result is insured, with no further claim.']),
190	    commonMistake: { mistake: 'Replacing not good with bad, or not required with forbidden.', repair: 'Deny the exact claim. Leave middle cases and different modal claims available.' },
191	    drills: {
192	      beginner: [
193	        question('negation-b1', 'Negate: The corridor is wide.', ['The corridor is narrow.', 'The corridor is not wide.', 'The corridor is short.'], 1, 'Do not replace the predicate with a stronger opposite.', ['Not wide denies exactly wide.', 'Narrow and short are additional claims.']),
194	        question('negation-b2', 'Negate: This badge is valid.', ['This badge is not valid.', 'No badge is valid.', 'This badge is old.'], 0, 'Keep this one badge as the subject.', ['The original concerns this badge alone.', 'Its denial is that this badge is not valid.']),
195	        question('negation-b3', 'Negate: The key is not missing.', ['The key is found.', 'The key is missing.', 'The key is not lost.'], 1, 'Remove the negative you are denying.', ['Not missing is the full predicate.', 'Its negation is missing; found adds a different claim.']),
196	      ],
197	      medium: [
198	        question('negation-m1', 'Negate: Permit P is valid for night access.', ['P is invalid for all access.', 'P is not valid for night access.', 'P is valid for daytime access.'], 1, 'Retain the night-access restriction.', ['Only validity for night access is denied.', 'Other permissions remain unsettled.']),
199	        question('negation-m2', 'Negate: Rowan must submit a report.', ['Rowan must not submit a report.', 'It is not required that Rowan submit a report.', 'Rowan did not submit a report.'], 1, 'Deny the requirement, not the action.', ['Not required removes the obligation.', 'It neither prohibits a report nor establishes noncompliance.']),
200	        question('negation-m3', 'Negate: The route is not unsafe.', ['The route is unsafe.', 'The route is safe.', 'The route is not safe.'], 0, 'Unsafe is the predicate inside not.', ['Denying not unsafe yields unsafe.', 'No separate definition equating safe with not unsafe is needed.']),
201	      ],
202	      advanced: [
203	        question('negation-a1', 'Negate: Shipment T arrived by 4 p.m. on Monday.', ['T arrived after 4 p.m. Monday.', 'T did not arrive by 4 p.m. Monday.', 'T arrived Tuesday.'], 1, 'Nonarrival must remain a possible way to deny the original.', ['The exact denial retains the same deadline.', 'Arrival later is only one possibility; nonarrival also fits.']),
204	        question('negation-a2', 'Negate: Device J is certified to operate at temperatures below zero.', ['J cannot operate below zero.', 'J is not certified to operate below zero.', 'J is certified only above zero.'], 1, 'Certification and physical ability differ.', ['The original is about a certification.', 'Denying it establishes neither inability nor another certification.']),
205	        question('negation-a3', 'Negate: It is not the case that Record Q is absent from the index.', ['Q is absent from the index.', 'Q is absent from every record system.', 'Q is present in the index.'], 0, 'First identify the claim inside the outer not.', ['The original denies absence from this index.', 'Negating that denial restores absence from this same index.']),
206	      ],
207	    },
208	  },
209	  {
210	    id: 'contraposition', title: 'Follow a Missing Requirement Backward', area: 'inference', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ['negation', 'sufficient-necessary'],
211	    goal: 'Form an equivalent rule by reversing the conditions and negating both.',
212	    teaching: [
213	      'Every verified invoice is recorded. If an invoice were verified, it would have to be recorded. An invoice that is not recorded therefore cannot be verified. Verified → recorded and not recorded → not verified make the same guarantee.',
214	      'To form a contrapositive, reverse the two complete conditions and negate both. Reversing alone gives an unsupported converse. Negating both without reversing gives an unsupported inverse.',
215	      'Keep existing negatives and cancel double negatives. If a sensor is active, it is not silent becomes if it is silent, it is not active. Keep the same device and time throughout. A normative requirement establishes what must be done, not automatic factual compliance.',
216	      'After forming a contrapositive, translate it back to English. Do not stop at symbols. For listed → not discontinued, its contrapositive is discontinued → not listed. Say it plainly: if this product is discontinued, it is not listed. That sentence checks both the negative and the direction.',
217	      'Use the diagramming routine to learn and check translation. It is a training scaffold, not extra writing required on every LSAT question. Apply an obvious forward match directly. Write the contrapositive when the fact denies a result or when it exposes a hidden connection. Use notation when it makes the relationship easier to track.',
218	    ],
219	    method: ['Write both complete conditions.', 'Exchange their positions.', 'Negate each condition, cancelling double negatives.', 'Read the new rule in ordinary language and check the missing-required-result reasoning.'],
220	    workedExample: { statements: ['Every listed product is not discontinued.'], reasoning: ['Listed → not discontinued.', 'Reverse and negate: discontinued → not listed.'], conclusion: 'Every discontinued product is not listed.' },
221	    guidedExample: question('contraposition-guide', 'Every restored clock keeps time. Which is equivalent?', ['Every timekeeping clock is restored.', 'Every clock not keeping time is not restored.', 'Every unrestored clock fails to keep time.'], 1, 'Begin with absence of the required result.', ['Keeping time is required for restoration under this rule.', 'A clock lacking it cannot be restored.']),
222	    commonMistake: { mistake: 'Reversing the arrow but leaving both conditions unchanged.', repair: 'Reverse and negate both. Check that the new trigger is the absence of the old required result.' },
223	    drills: {
224	      beginner: [
225	        question('contraposition-b1', 'Every sealed envelope is stamped. Its contrapositive is:', ['Stamped → sealed.', 'Not stamped → not sealed.', 'Not sealed → not stamped.'], 1, 'Reverse the conditions and negate both.', ['Stamping is required for sealing in this rule.', 'No stamp therefore excludes sealing.']),
226	        question('contraposition-b2', 'If a lamp is on, it draws current. The lamp draws no current. What follows?', ['The lamp is not on.', 'The lamp is broken.', 'The lamp has no bulb.'], 0, 'Use the missing required result, without inventing a cause.', ['Being on would require current.', 'No current excludes on, but does not establish why.']),
227	        question('contraposition-b3', 'Every checked form is complete. Which is the unsupported inverse?', ['Not complete → not checked.', 'Checked → complete.', 'Not checked → not complete.'], 2, 'The inverse negates without swapping.', ['The original starts with checked.', 'Not checked → not complete adds a direction the original does not guarantee.']),
228	      ],

242	    id: 'group-1', title: "Group 1: Identify What Guarantees What", area: 'translation', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ["sufficient-necessary"],
243	    goal: "Find the complete sufficient idea in each Group 1 expression and preserve its meaning in a diagram.",
244	    teaching: [
245	      "We've understood enough and required. Now let's see how different words express the sufficient side. Group 1 includes if, when, where, all, the only, every and any in their conditional uses. The words help you find a relationship. You still need to read what the whole sentence says.",
246	      "Start with if: If an order is marked ready, it has been packed. If what? The order is marked ready. That's the sufficient idea. Now move the clause: An order has been packed if it is marked ready. The same idea still follows if, and the same guarantee runs from marked ready to packed. The first words on the page aren't always the left side of the diagram.",
247	      "When can introduce the event that's enough: When the alarm sounds, the staff evacuate. Ask when what. The alarm sounds. Under this rule, that event guarantees evacuation. Whenever makes the same repeated-case meaning explicit. But When I checked the desk, a notice was there reports one occasion; it doesn't promise a notice every time I check.",
248	      "Where can make a rule about a location or situation: Where a station is available, the service operates. Which locations does the rule cover? Locations with an available station. At one of those locations, the service has to operate. Keep the location connection when you read the rule back; a station elsewhere hasn't met this location's condition.",
249	      "All covers the whole group it names: All registered guests receive a wristband. All what? Registered guests, not guests generally. Being one of those registered guests is enough for a wristband. Another guest may receive one too; the sentence hasn't told us. It also hasn't told us that a registered guest exists.",
250	      "The only needs careful reading: The only vehicles permitted inside are electric vehicles. The only what? Vehicles permitted inside. If a vehicle is permitted inside, it must be electric. Try electric vehicle → permitted inside. That would grant permission to every electric vehicle, which the sentence never promised. The grammatical target is the whole permission description, not the word electric.",
251	      "Every takes the members one at a time but covers them all: Every finalist receives an invitation. Choose a finalist. That person has to receive an invitation under the rule. All finalists receive an invitation would express the same relationship. Neither wording says everyone receiving an invitation is a finalist.",
252	      "Any says that whichever member of the named group you choose, the rule applies: Any applicant who submits late pays a fee. Choose a late-submitting applicant and that person pays a fee under the rule. Choose an applicant who submits on time and this sentence doesn't settle the fee question. Submits late stays with applicant in your diagram.",
253	      "The What Test keeps you with the complete idea: if what, when what, where what, all what, the only what, every what, or any what? Don't stop at the nearest noun. In A report appears when a rover crosses the ridge, the target is the rover crossing the ridge. That event goes left, even though the sentence begins with the report.",
254	      "An indicator can have another job. The curator asked if the painting was insured tells us about a question the curator asked. It gives no guarantee between asking and insurance. Read for a conditional meaning before drawing an arrow.",
255	      "To build the diagram, name both complete ideas and give them clear condensed labels. Place the sufficient idea on the left and what it guarantees on the right. 256	      "For Recall and Rephrase, put the list aside and try a fresh sentence. Write its if/then meaning, then compare with the original. The point is to keep the relationship when the wording changes, not merely to remember which side a word usually points toward.",

304	  {
305	    id: 'group-2', title: "Group 2: Keep the Requirement on the Right", area: 'translation', phase: 'conditional', prerequisites: ["group-1"],
306	    goal: "Find what a Group 2 sentence requires, preserve its complete meaning, and distinguish a requirement from success or compliance.",
307	    teaching: [
308	      "Group 2 helps us find the necessary side: what the sentence says is required. Its core expressions are only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must in their relevant uses. We'll keep the complete requirement on the right. First, let's hear why it belongs there.",
309	      "Compare if with only if. An order is marked ready if it has been packed makes packing enough for ready status. An order is marked ready only if it has been packed says packing is required for ready status. Those aren't the same rule. In the second sentence, a packed order could still be waiting for a ready label. The sentence permits that possibility.",
310	      "Only restricts who or what can have the other condition: Only packed orders are marked ready. If an order is marked ready, it must be packed. Only what? Packed orders. Compare The only orders marked ready are packed orders. That Group 1 form reaches the same ready → packed relationship, but its target is orders marked ready. Read the phrase with its grammar rather than treating only and the only as interchangeable labels.",
311	      "Only if introduces a condition that has to be met: A student graduates only if the student completes the project. If the student graduates, the project must be completed. Project completion by itself doesn't promise graduation. Read the reverse aloud and you'll hear the extra guarantee it adds.",
312	      "Only when makes an event or state required: A review occurs only when a complaint is filed. A complaint has to be filed for this review to occur. Imagine a complaint has been filed. Does this sentence say a review now occurs? No. It says what a review requires, not that every complaint gets one.",
313	      "Only where makes a location condition required: The service operates only where a station is available. Wherever the service operates, a station has to be available there. A station at another location isn't enough. Nor has the sentence promised service to every place with an available station.",
314	      "Always describes the result that holds throughout the named cases: A finalist always receives an invitation. Start with a finalist. Under that claim, the person receives an invitation. The subject supplies the sufficient idea; receiving an invitation is the required result. Always belongs in this teaching group, but it doesn't mean the next word automatically goes on the right. Read the complete subject and result.",
315	      "Requires tells you what is needed: Entry requires a current pass. Requires what? A current pass. Entry goes left, having the current pass goes right. Change the wording to A current pass is required for entry. The requirement appears first now, but the relationship hasn't changed. A pass alone isn't a promise that you can enter, and an expired pass doesn't meet the full requirement.",
316	      "Must can state an obligation: A finalist must attend the interview. What does finalist status establish? The person is required to attend. It doesn't establish that the person actually attends; people can fail to meet an obligation. Write Finalist → Required to attend interview so the diagram preserves that difference. Keep permission words such as may in the same careful way.",
317	      "Use the What Test on the full expression, then check with a Personalized If/Then. If my display has activated, what does the battery rule force me to know? Its battery is charged. Now suppose I know only that the battery is charged. The rule still leaves activation open. That comparison checks the arrow against the sentence's ordinary meaning.",
318	      "Keep every part of a requirement. In Entry requires a current pass, pass would drop current. In Only members may use this room, actually uses the room would change permission into an action. The right direction can't repair a label that has lost part of the idea.",
319	      "In the fresh practice, explain what is required and what remains open. If your answer treats a requirement as enough, try saying that answer as an if/then. Compare it with the original sentence and point to the extra promise. That's the mistake we're learning to catch.",
320	    ],

323	      "Name both complete ideas. Keep restrictions, negatives, permission and obligation in their meaning.",
324	      "Choose clear condensed labels. Required to attend must not become actually attends.",
325	      "Place the restricted event or condition left and its requirement right. Check that direction with a Personalized If/Then.",
326	      "Read the arrow back in English. Compare the reverse too: did the original sentence really make the requirement enough?",

6	const TABLE = 'conditional_course_state';

8	const localStores = new Map<string, LocalStoragePersistence>();
9	function localStore(userId: string) {
10	  let store = localStores.get(userId);
11	  if (!store) {
12	    // The shared IndexedDB adapter has fixed MC stores and unscoped record IDs.
13	    // The generic local adapter scopes every conditional key to this account.
14	    store = new LocalStoragePersistence(userId);
15	    localStores.set(userId, store);
16	  }
17	  return store;
18	}
19	export function useConditionalProgress(userId: string) {
20	  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);
21	  const [savedProgress, setSavedProgress] = useState<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);
22	  const [status, setStatus] = useState<'loading' | 'unsaved' | 'saving' | 'saved' | 'error'>('loading');
23	  const [error, setError] = useState('');
24	  const [loadedActor, setLoadedActor] = useState<string | null>(null);
25	  const current = useRef<ConditionalProgress | null>(null);
26	  const revision = useRef(0);
27	  const persistedRevision = useRef(0);
28	  const generation = useRef(0);
29	  const controller = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);
30	  const inFlight = useRef(false);
31	  const localQueue = useRef(Promise.resolve());
32	  const flushRef = useRef<() => Promise<void>>();
33	  const cloudRevision = useRef<string | null>(null);
34	
35	  const flush = useCallback(async () => {
36	    if (inFlight.current || !current.current || revision.current === persistedRevision.current) return;
37	    const actor = generation.current;
38	    inFlight.current = true;
39	    setStatus('saving');
40	    try {
41	      while (actor === generation.current && revision.current !== persistedRevision.current) {
42	        const version = revision.current;
43	        const snapshot = current.current;
44	        const writer = cloudRevision.current
45	          ? supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').update({ data: snapshot as never }).eq('user_id', userId).eq('table_name', TABLE).eq('record_id', RECORD).eq('updated_at', cloudRevision.current)
46	          : supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').insert({ user_id: userId, table_name: TABLE, record_id: RECORD, data: snapshot as never });
47	        const { data: acknowledgement, error: saveError } = await writer.select('record_id,updated_at').abortSignal(controller.current.signal).maybeSingle();
48	        if (saveError) {
49	          if (saveError.code === '23505') throw new Error('Another window saved this course first. Your draft is preserved on this device; ask Joshua to reconcile the two versions.');
50	          throw saveError;
51	        }
52	        if (acknowledgement?.record_id !== RECORD || !acknowledgement.updated_at) throw new Error('Your saved work changed in another window. This draft is preserved on this device. Reload to reconcile before continuing; it will not overwrite the other window.');
53	        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
54	        const acknowledgedBase = cloudRevision.current;
55	        cloudRevision.current = acknowledgement.updated_at;
56	        // New edits queued while this snapshot was in flight still have its old
57	        // base. Advance only that base after the acknowledgement, preserving the
58	        // newest draft contents and the order of any subsequent local writes.
59	        const nextBase = acknowledgement.updated_at;
60	        localQueue.current = localQueue.current.catch(() => undefined).then(async () => {
61	          if (actor !== generation.current) return;
62	          const base = await localStore(userId).get<{ revision: string | null }>('conditional_draft_base', RECORD);
63	          if (base?.revision === acknowledgedBase) {
64	            await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_base', RECORD, { revision: nextBase });
65	          }
66	        });
67	        await localQueue.current;
68	        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
69	        await localStore(userId).set(TABLE, RECORD, snapshot);
70	        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
71	        persistedRevision.current = version;
72	        setSavedProgress(snapshot);
73	      }
74	      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('saved'); setError(''); }
75	    } catch (failure) {
76	      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('error'); setError(failure instanceof Error ? failure.message : 'Your changes have not been saved. Retry while this page stays open.'); }
77	    } finally {
78	      if (actor === generation.current) inFlight.current = false;
79	    }
80	  }, [userId]);
81	  flushRef.current = flush;
82	
83	  useEffect(() => {
84	    const actor = ++generation.current;
85	    const actorController = new AbortController();
86	    controller.current = actorController;
87	    current.current = null;
88	    revision.current = 0;
89	    persistedRevision.current = 0;
90	    inFlight.current = false;
91	    setProgress(null);
92	    setSavedProgress(null);
93	    setLoadedActor(null);
94	    setStatus('loading');
95	    void (async () => {
96	      try {
97	        const persistence = localStore(userId);
98	        await persistence.ready();
99	        // A failed initial cloud read must not manufacture defaults over account progress.
100	        const { data, error: readError } = await supabase.from('bootcamp_kv').select('data,updated_at').eq('user_id', userId).eq('table_name', TABLE).eq('record_id', RECORD).abortSignal(actorController.signal).maybeSingle();
101	        if (readError) throw readError;
102	        const saved = data?.data != null ? parseConditionalProgress(data.data) : undefined;
103	        const rawLocal = await persistence.get<unknown>('conditional_draft_state', RECORD);
104	        const local = rawLocal != null ? parseConditionalProgress(rawLocal) : null;
105	        const dirty = local && (!saved || Date.parse(local.updated_at) > Date.parse(saved.updated_at));
106	        const draftBase = await persistence.get<{ revision: string | null }>('conditional_draft_base', RECORD);
107	        if (dirty && draftBase && draftBase.revision !== (data?.updated_at ?? null)) throw new Error('This device has unfinished work based on an older account version. Both versions are preserved. Ask Joshua to reconcile them before continuing.');
108	        const value = dirty ? local : saved ?? emptyProgress();
109	        if (actor !== generation.current) return;
110	        current.current = value;
111	        cloudRevision.current = data?.updated_at ?? null;
112	        setProgress(value);
113	        setSavedProgress(saved ?? emptyProgress());
114	        setLoadedActor(userId);
115	        if (dirty) revision.current = 1;
116	        setStatus(dirty ? 'unsaved' : 'saved');
117	      } catch (failure) {
118	        if (actor === generation.current) { setLoadedActor(userId); setStatus('error'); setError(failure instanceof Error ? failure.message : 'Could not load your saved work. Reload to try again.'); }
119	      }
120	    })();
121	    return () => { generation.current = actor + 1; actorController.abort(); };
122	  }, [userId]);
123	
124	  useEffect(() => {
125	    if (status !== 'unsaved') return;
126	    const timer = setTimeout(() => void flushRef.current?.(), 350);
127	    return () => clearTimeout(timer);
128	  }, [progress, status]);
129	
130	  useEffect(() => {
131	    const beforeUnload = (event: BeforeUnloadEvent) => {
132	      if (revision.current === persistedRevision.current) return;
133	      event.preventDefault();
134	      event.returnValue = '';
135	    };
136	    window.addEventListener('beforeunload', beforeUnload);
137	    return () => window.removeEventListener('beforeunload', beforeUnload);
138	  }, []);
139	
140	  const update = useCallback((transform: (previous: ConditionalProgress) => ConditionalProgress) => {
141	    if (!current.current || loadedActor !== userId) return;
142	    const value = { ...transform(current.current), updated_at: new Date().toISOString() };
143	    current.current = value;
144	    revision.current++;
145	    setProgress(value);
146	    setStatus(inFlight.current ? 'saving' : 'unsaved');
147	    const actor = generation.current;
148	    const baseRevision = cloudRevision.current;
149	    localQueue.current = localQueue.current.catch(() => undefined).then(async () => {
150	      await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_state', RECORD, value);
151	      await localStore(userId).set('conditional_draft_base', RECORD, { revision: baseRevision });
152	    }).catch(() => {
153	      if (actor === generation.current) { setStatus('error'); setError('Could not preserve this draft on your device. Keep this page open and retry saving.'); }
154	    });
155	  }, [userId, loadedActor]);
156	  return { progress: loadedActor === userId ? progress : null, savedProgress: loadedActor === userId ? savedProgress : null, update, status: loadedActor === userId ? status : 'loading' as const, error: loadedActor === userId ? error : '', retry: flush };
157	}

1	import { useState } from 'react';
2	import { useSearchParams } from 'react-router-dom';
3	import { ConditionalLessonNote } from '@/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalLessonNote';
4	import { findConditionalUnit } from '@/bootcamps/conditionals/content';
5	
6	/** Isolated development harness. It does not mount assignment or account persistence. */
7	export default function ConditionalOpeningPreview() {
8	  const [params, setParams] = useSearchParams();
9	  const [reviewed, setReviewed] = useState(false);
10	  const id = params.get('unit') ?? 'orientation';
11	  const ids = ['orientation', 'sufficient-necessary', 'group-1', 'group-2'];
12	  const unit = findConditionalUnit(ids.includes(id) ? id : 'orientation')!;
13	  const next = ids[(ids.indexOf(unit.id) + 1) % ids.length];
14	  return <main style={{ background: '#e9e7dd', minHeight: '100vh', padding: '20px 12px 70px' }}><nav aria-label="Development lesson preview" style={{ maxWidth: 1060, margin: '0 auto 20px', display: 'flex', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: 12 }}>{ids.map(value => <button key={value} style={{ padding: '10px 15px' }} aria-pressed={value === unit.id} onClick={() => { setParams({ unit: value }); setReviewed(false); }}>{value}</button>)}</nav><ConditionalLessonNote unit={unit} reviewed={reviewed} onComplete={() => setReviewed(true)} continueHref={`/dev/conditional-opening-preview?unit=${next}`} continueLabel="Preview next lesson"/></main>;
15	}
